# Needs Claude Code

- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code, confirmed by Mike in Play Mode) — see Log.** ~~**Feature: Jump.**~~ New movement state for First_Person_Controller.md, confirmed 2026-09-25 (Mike, playtest) — the controller currently has no jump at all (Walk/Sprint/Crouch/Swim-future only). Exact jump height, air control, and Stamina cost are Claude Code's call; see the new Jump section in First_Person_Controller.md, right after Crouch. **Resolved 2026-09-25 — Mike confirmed jumping works well in Play Mode.** No implementation report came through this channel (built and tested directly between Mike and Claude Code), so exact jump height/air control/Stamina cost aren't recorded here — worth a follow-up note in First_Person_Controller.md's Jump section next time Claude Code documents it, but not blocking.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest.** ~~**Feature: Foraging.**~~ One of the 8 still-missing GDD-required Alpha 0.1 systems. Foraging_System.md is a fully written Design Draft: five resource categories (Berries, Fruit, Nuts, Edible Plants, Mushrooms), Discovery integration reusing the existing marker pattern (🫐 Berry Patch, 🌰 Nut Grove, 🍎 Fruit Tree, 🍄 Mushroom Area), seasonal availability (best in Summer/Fall, minimal in Winter), and harvest-then-regrow-next-same-season regeneration. Requesting forage nodes placed in World that the player interacts with to harvest (same "[Hold E]" interaction pattern already used for drinking and item pickup), each tied to a `DiscoverySite` so the first harvest fires the existing discovery notification, producing new Resource-category items into Inventory. Nodes deplete on harvest and regrow on the doc's seasonal cadence. Exact node placement/count, yield per visit, and regrowth timing (real days vs. in-game season boundary) aren't locked by the doc and are Claude Code's call. Not blocked on Fire Building — per the doc, foraged food can be eaten fresh (see the Eating entry below). **How it plays:** 13 patches, one per species, placed by habitat — berries on forest edges, the apple tree in the old field, nuts and mushrooms deep in the woods, dandelions and onions in open pasture, cattails on the pond shore; the existing Briar Patch site is now the blackberry patch. Looking at a patch shows what it is and when it bears ("Harvest Wild Apples (4)", "picked, bears again next Fall", "bears in Early Spring") — ripe fruit/nuts/mushrooms only show while there's something to pick. First harvest discovers the patch (popup, journal entry, its own map icon). Yields and regrowth follow the Plants docs — most patches come back next year; dandelion, onion, cattail, and the two log mushrooms regrow in about 3–4 days; Morels only appear in a narrow early-Spring window. Tested: real harvests, discovery, picked-state saving/loading, dandelions regrowing after 4 days, and the morel window opening/closing on time.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest.** ~~**Feature: Fishing, including Fish Traps.**~~ Two of the 8 still-missing GDD-required systems, named separately on the list but specced together in Fishing_System.md as active vs. passive methods of one system. Rod and Reel and Cane Pole are the active methods; Fish Traps are the passive method (build → place → wait → check → harvest — ineffective on Bass, especially good on Catfish). Four species, each with its own doc under `05_Documentation/Fishing/` (Bluegill, Catfish, Bass, Crappie) covering species-specific yield, behavior, and seasonal windows (e.g. Crappie's Spring Spawn Window). Bass Hole, one of the five already-placed Discovery_Test_Sites, is ready to fish against. Requesting a first-pass active fishing interaction (cast/wait for a bite/reel in — exact input and timing Claude Code's call) plus the fish trap build/place/check loop, both producing the four species as new Resource-category food items. How deeply to model the doc's Season and Weather Effects sections on catch rate in this first pass is also Claude Code's call — fine to start simple and tune later, same as the other propose-then-confirm systems. **How it plays:** click to cast the Rod or Cane Pole at water, wait for a bite (the bobber dips with a plop), click to set the hook, then hold to reel — ease off when the fish runs or the line snaps. Which fish bites depends on place, time and season: Bass by day at the Bass Hole, Crappie and Catfish at night, Crappie flooding in during Spring (the spring pool itself holds no fish). Catches give each species' Item_Data.md units (Bluegill 1, Crappie 2, Bass 3, Catfish 4) and get noted in the Bass Hole journal entry. Fish Trap: build it (Inventory → Build), equip it, click to set it in water, then check with E — it catches hour by hour, mostly Catfish and Bluegill, rarely Bass. Tested: a full cast → bite → reel loop landing a real bass, and a fish trap catching a crappie overnight. **Update 2026-09-25 (Mike, playtest):** the line snaps way too quickly while reeling in. Fishing_System.md never locked any tension/snap numbers — this was Claude Code's own first-pass tuning, flagged from the start as fine to start simple and tune later — so this is a straightforward tuning ask: give more room before the line snaps (wider tolerance on how hard/long the player can pull during a run, and/or a longer window to ease off), no design change needed. Exact numbers are Claude Code's call, same propose-then-confirm pattern as everything else in this batch. **Resolved 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — committed and pushed (`96ea709`), confirmed by Mike in Play Mode.** Diagnosed the old tuning as the real cause: reeling for even a quarter-second into a fish's run built up damage, the line broke after half a second of that, and the damage only faded on a full release — a normal ~0.4s human reaction snapped every Bass and Catfish and nearly half of Crappie. New tuning: the first 0.2s of each run is a free reaction window before any damage accrues, the line now takes 0.8s of sustained pulling through runs to break (up from 0.5s), tension fades a little while reeling between runs and much faster once the player lets go, and the status line now shows a live "Line tension X%" readout so the player can see when to back off. Simulated over 300–400 reels per fish before Mike's own test: Bluegill/Crappie/Bass/Catfish all 0% lost to a snapped line with a normal 0.4s reaction, vs. 45%/100%/100% before for Crappie/Bass/Catfish; even never easing off at all still lands every Bluegill and Crappie, with Bass/Catfish (the hardest fighters) still losable at 44%/86% if the player just holds the button through everything, keeping some skill in landing the big ones. All four tuning numbers are adjustable on the Player prefab's Fishing Rod component if the feel still needs a nudge. Also fixed in passing: a bug where putting the rod away with no audio system running (playing the World scene directly, not from the main menu) threw an error on the reel sound. Mike played it and confirmed it feels better; committed alongside the Aiming work in `96ea709` (doc updates in `5f68600`), both pushed to `master`.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest.** ~~**Feature: Hunting.**~~ Scope note: Hunting isn't one of the GDD's named Alpha 0.1 Required Systems (only Foraging, Fishing, and Fish Traps are) — but Mike asked for it directly alongside those, and Hunting_System.md is already a complete Design Draft, so it's being built now rather than held back. Specs the Recurve Bow (early, silent, short range, real wounding risk) and Bolt-Action Rifle (longer range, more reliable, loud); three yield tiers — Small Game (rabbit/squirrel), Medium Game (turkey/waterfowl), Large Game (deer, with hide and antlers) — with full species behavior in `05_Documentation/Wildlife/*.md`; Clean Kill/Wounding Hit/Miss outcomes; field dressing speed affecting meat quality and spoilage (ties to Core_Survival_System.md's Spoilage System); Fall as the primary season. Requesting a first-pass hunting interaction: aim and shoot with an equipped weapon (ties into the Inventory screen's existing Tool-equip), a hit outcome influenced by shot placement/distance, and field dressing as a follow-up interaction on a downed animal within some time window. Exact shot mechanics (hitscan vs. projectile, aim assist) and the field-dressing time window are Claude Code's call — the doc explicitly defers Wounded Animal Tracking itself to a Future System, so a wounding hit should just mean a reduced or lost harvest for Alpha 0.1, not a tracking minigame. Trapping is its own sibling entry below. **How it plays:** wildlife by species and time — deer at dawn/dusk, and all day during the second half of Fall (the rut); turkey at dawn; rabbits and squirrels by day; ducks on the pond once Bass Hole is discovered, gone in Winter. Heavy weather halves how many animals are out. Stealth matters: deer hear walking at 32m, crouching at 13m, sprinting at 64m, with wind carrying scent (±70% detection range) and rain masking sound. The Recurve Bow is hold-to-draw/release-to-fire, accurate to about 30m, and recovers its arrows from a kill; the Bolt-Action Rifle is click-to-fire with a bolt-cycle pause between shots, accurate to about 150m, but its noise spooks anything within 250m. A hit to heart or lungs is a clean kill; anywhere else is a wounding hit with reduced or lost harvest. Field dressing is a race against time: within 1 hour gives full meat, by 3 hours it's down to 3/4 meat and a day of shelf life lost, by 8 hours half meat and two days lost, and after that only hide/antlers/feathers/fur remain. Hide comes from any deer; antlers are more likely from bucks during the Fall rut. Meat past what the player can carry stays on the carcass, and a shot duck drifts to the bank instead of sinking. Tested: a rifle clean kill with a 2-hour dressing wait (6 venison + hide, with 1 venison left behind because the pack was full), a bow kill with arrow recovery, and a duck retrieval (3 meat + 2 feathers). **Update 2026-09-25 (Mike, playtest):** it was hard to tell where a shot would land at a deer — wants a scope or sights added for aiming. Requesting an aiming reticle/sight picture for both weapons; exact treatment per weapon (a simple crosshair for the Bow vs. real aim-down-sights or a slight-zoom scope for the Rifle) is Claude Code's call — see the new Aiming section in Hunting_System.md. **Resolved 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — committed and pushed (`96ea709`), confirmed by Mike in Play Mode.** Bow gets a spread reticle, Rifle gets real aim-down-sights/scope, both behind a new dedicated Aim action (right-click / left trigger), plus an amber/green shot-quality color cue on the reticle. Bundled in the same commit as the fishing line-tension retune below; doc updates went in a companion commit (`5f68600`). Mike played it and confirmed the sighting/aiming works well.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest.** ~~**Feature: Trapping.**~~ Same scope note as Hunting: not one of the GDD's named Alpha 0.1 Required Systems, but Mike's asked for it alongside the rest of this food batch, and Trapping_System.md is already a complete Design Draft — Hunting_System.md itself even lists Trapping as one of "the five primary Year One food sources, alongside Foraging, Fishing, Fish Traps, and Trapping," so this rounds that set out. Two early trap types: Rabbit Snare (needs Cordage, placed on rabbit trails/brush edges) and Box Trap (needs Wood and Bait, placed near food sources). Core loop matches Fish Traps' shape: discover animal activity → place trap → bait it → wait → check → harvest. Catch results are deliberately unpredictable — No Catch / Small Catch / Successful Catch / Trap Damage / Bait Consumed — and traps need periodic maintenance (check/repair/re-bait) or effectiveness drops. Yields Meat and Small Furs from Rabbit and Squirrel. Requesting placeable trap objects using the doc's material/bait requirements, a check interaction that rolls one of the five catch results, and maintenance state that degrades if a trap is left unchecked. Ties into Discovery the same way as the other gathering systems — successful trapping reveals animal trail/feeding-area sites per the doc. Exact roll odds, degrade timing, and how placement quality (animal traffic, season, nearby food, weather) affects the roll aren't locked by the doc and are Claude Code's call, same propose-then-confirm pattern as the rest of this batch. **How it plays:** build a Rabbit Snare (1 Cordage) or Box Trap (3 Firewood) from Inventory → Build, equip it, then click to set it on the ground — spots near brush/forest edges or a discovered animal trail favor a snare, spots near food favor a box trap, and the game hints at spot quality when placing. A Box Trap needs bait (apples, berries, pawpaw, or greens) to work well. Checking a trap with E rolls one of five results: No Catch, a Small Catch (meat only), a Successful Catch (meat and fur), Trap Damage, or Bait Consumed with nothing caught. Traps wear about 10%/day and break if left unchecked too long; R repairs one with Cordage or Firewood, or picks it up. In Fall, a well-placed, undisturbed snare catches on roughly 60% of days, a baited box trap around 55%, and an unbaited one only about 11%. Tested: both a snare and a box trap caught a rabbit within two days, plus repair, pick-up, and save/load all working correctly.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest; fuel numbers and the starting kit are first proposals.** ~~**Feature: Fire Building.**~~ One of the 8 still-missing GDD-required Alpha 0.1 systems, and per Core_Survival_System.md's own Core Principles ("Fire Enables Survival") and Core Survival Loop (Water → Fire → Safe Water → Shelter → Food...), it's the system the two entries below now depend on directly. The Fire System section specs four functions — water purification, food cooking, warmth, lighting, food preservation — and three requirements: fuel, an ignition source, and maintenance. Requesting a placeable campfire (built from a Wood/fuel item, exact recipe Claude Code's call since the doc doesn't lock one), an ignition interaction, and a burn-down-over-time state that needs fuel added periodically or it goes out, matching the doc's "maintenance" requirement. This entry is just the fire itself — Cooking and Water Purification below are what actually use it. **Update 2026-09-25 (Mike, playtest):** first playtest went well, campfire looks good. One gap: right now a lit campfire can only be fed more fuel — there's no way to voluntarily put it out. Wants an extinguish interaction on a lit campfire so unused firewood can be conserved rather than burning off unattended, with the same built campfire object then relightable later using the same ignition interaction/item, instead of needing to build a new one from scratch. Exact interaction (a key prompt like the existing Tend/Build ones, doused instantly vs. a short burn-down animation) is Claude Code's call. **Resolved 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest.** A lit campfire now shows a second prompt, "[R] Put Out Fire," a hold like E so it can't be triggered by accident — a new rebindable `Player/InteractAlt` action. Putting it out keeps every hour of remaining fuel and the logs, and the flames/light/crackle fade over 1.5s rather than snapping off. Relighting is the same "[E] Light Fire" with Flint and Steel as before.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built and tested, NOT YET committed.** ~~**Feature: Cooking.**~~ Mike's flagged that not everything can be eaten raw. Foraging_System.md explicitly allows berries/fruit/nuts to be "eaten fresh," but Core_Survival_System.md's Spoilage System treats Raw Fish and Raw Meat as their own categories with their own shelf lives, and lists Illness as one of Health's five inputs — read together, that means meat and fish should carry a real illness risk eaten raw, the same way under-purified water does in the entry below, while produce doesn't need this. Neither doc locks exact numbers, so this is Claude Code's call to propose and Mike to confirm by feel, same pattern as the rest of this batch. Requesting a Cook interaction at a lit campfire (from the Fire Building entry) that turns a raw Meat/Fish item into a Cooked version — a new Consumable variant with no illness risk and, per the doc's Food Preservation section, a longer shelf life than raw. The Eating entry below should treat raw Meat/Fish as edible but risky, and cooked Meat/Fish as the safe, better option — this is what actually makes cooking matter rather than just being flavor text. **How it plays:** stand within 3m of a lit campfire and open Inventory — raw meat/fish rows get a Cook button (Shift-click cooks the whole stack), and the campfire's own prompt now adds "Cook or boil from Inventory (I)" when carrying something cookable. Nine new Cooked Consumable items were added (venison, turkey, waterfowl, chicken, small game, bluegill, crappie, bass, catfish), each a bit lighter than raw with no illness risk and a longer shelf life — cooked meat keeps 5 days (raw 3), cooked fish 4 (raw 2) — and roughly 4/3 the food value of raw (cooked venison 40 vs. raw 30). Cooking is instant for now, no time cost and no extra firewood burned; Claude Code flagged this as open to change if Mike would rather it take real time. Tested: does nothing at an unlit fire; cooked 1 venison once lit; Shift-cooked both bluegill at once; apples aren't cookable. **Resolved 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — built and tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (restored and hash-checked after), NOT yet committed or pushed.** Bundled with Eating and Water Purification below in the same Phase 3 batch — Claude Code is holding off on committing until Mike says the word. **Update 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — cooking is no longer instant, still NOT committed.** Mike apparently told Claude Code directly (not relayed through this channel) that he'd rather cooking take real time — implemented as a per-item timer rather than instant, though fuel/firewood consumption isn't mentioned in this report so presumably that part is unchanged. First-pass seconds-per-item (for Mike to judge by feel, adjustable on the Player prefab's Inspector): Fish 4s, Small game/chicken 5s, Turkey/waterfowl 6s, Venison 8s, boiling water 6s per litre. How it plays: clicking Cook or Boil in Inventory starts cooking one, showing a countdown like "62% (1)"; Shift-click queues the whole stack; clicking again cancels. Cooking keeps running while the Inventory screen is open (even though the game clock itself is paused there), so you can stand and watch it; with Inventory closed, the HUD shows "Cooking Venison, 3 to go" with a progress bar. Walking away from the fire pauses cooking with "Cooking paused — get back to a burning campfire" — each raw piece is only consumed once it actually finishes, so nothing is lost, and it picks back up when you return. Tested: two bluegill and a litre of unsafe water finished in about 14s; venison cooked correctly with the menu open; stopping an item and walking 10m away paused it with nothing cooking while away; save restored and hash-checked after, project settings file unchanged. Still flagged: no dedicated cooking sound yet — each finished piece plays the normal pickup sound; Claude Code suggested Cooking.mp3 (still sitting in `Xtra Sound files/`) would suit a looping sizzle if Mike wants that polish.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Ready for Mike's playtest.** ~~**Feature: Water Collection.**~~ One of the 8 still-missing GDD-required systems. Water_System.md's early-game Water Transportation is Hand Carrying via Buckets or a Yoke and Buckets — available from Day One, no construction required (Wagon/Cart hauling and anything powered are explicitly Future, Not Alpha 0.1). Right now the player can only drink directly at a water source (the Hydration/Hunger entry's existing "[Hold E] Drink" interaction) — there's no way to carry water away from a source. Requesting a Bucket item/tool that can be filled at a water source (same interaction pattern as drinking) to produce a carryable "raw water" Consumable in Inventory, tagged with that source's Water Quality (see the Water Purification entry below) so it can be boiled or drunk later rather than only on the spot. Cistern/Rain Barrel storage is scoped out for now, same reasoning as Inventory's Home Storage — no buildable structures exist in Alpha 0.1 yet.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built and tested, NOT YET committed.** ~~**Feature: Water Purification.**~~ The last of the 8 still-missing GDD-required systems this batch covers, leaving only Tent Placement and Sleeping. Water_System.md grounds this precisely: four Water Quality tiers (Excellent — springs/wells, Very Low illness risk; Good — fast creeks/rain, Low risk; Questionable — slow streams, Moderate risk, purification recommended; Unsafe — stagnant ponds/floodwater, High risk, purification strongly recommended) and Boiling as the Alpha 0.1 purification method, requiring Fire (the entry above) and a Container. Right now every drink source is treated as equally safe with no risk modeled at all — per Claude Code's own Hydration/Hunger log, "water quality and illness risk aren't modelled, so every source is safe for now." Requesting: (a) each existing water source (the creek, the spring, the pond) gets a Water Quality tier per the doc's own examples — the spring should be Excellent, the creek Good, the pond Questionable-to-Unsafe; (b) drinking directly, or drinking collected raw water (from the Water Collection entry), below Excellent carries a real illness risk, same lightweight approach as Cooking's raw-meat risk above — not a full Illness System (Core_Survival_System.md explicitly defers "detailed diseases" to Future Expansion), just a real consequence for skipping purification; (c) a Boil interaction at a lit campfire that turns a collected raw-water item into a safe Purified Water item, no risk. Exact quality-to-risk odds and what the illness effect actually does (a temporary stamina/Health penalty, matching how Hydration's own low tiers already work, seems the natural fit) are Claude Code's call. **How it plays:** each tier now carries a real per-litre illness risk — Excellent 0%, Good 5%, Questionable 20%, Unsafe 45% — applying both to drinking straight from a source and to carried water. The drink prompt now warns you, e.g. "Drink (Good water, slight risk of sickness)." At a lit campfire, raw water rows get a Boil button (needs the Bucket); boiling produces Purified Water (0% risk) by reusing the old, previously-unused generic `water` item id, and it still counts toward the bucket's capacity. Getting sick triggers the new Sickness mechanic — see the corresponding update to Health_System.md's Illness System section. Tested: boiling Good water into Purified Water worked via both the dedicated Boil button and a click on the water row; 400 drinks from Good-quality creek water produced sickness 15 times (~4%), close to the 5% target. **Resolved 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — built and tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (restored and hash-checked after), NOT yet committed or pushed.** Bundled with Cooking above and Eating below in the same Phase 3 batch.
- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built and tested, NOT YET committed.** ~~**Feature: Eating (consuming food from Inventory).**~~ Closes the gap Claude Code flagged in its Hydration/Hunger log and ties directly into the already-built Inventory screen, whose rows are ready for a Use action with nothing yet to trigger it. Requesting a new food-effect on `ItemDefinition` (Hunger restored, and Hydration restored for things like fruit, per Core_Survival_System.md's six food categories: Meat, Fish, Fruits, Nuts, Vegetables, Preserved Foods) and an Eat/Use action on Consumable-category items in the Inventory screen that consumes one, applies its effect through `SurvivalManager`, and removes it from Inventory. Raw produce (berries, fruit, nuts) is fine eaten fresh per Foraging_System.md; raw Meat/Fish carries the illness risk described in the Cooking entry above, so cooking them first is the safe option once Fire Building/Cooking exist — not a hard requirement to eat at all, just the risk/reward the docs imply. Exact restore values per food type are a first-pass proposal for Mike to confirm by feel, same pattern as Hunger's own effect curve. Depends on Foraging/Fishing/Hunting/Trapping existing to produce real food items to test against, though the Use-action plumbing itself can be built and tested with placeholder items in the meantime. **Note 2026-09-25:** Mike's already sourced an eating sound (`eating.mp3`, in the repo's `Xtra Sound files/` folder) — see Audio_System.md's new sfx_eating row and Sourced Files note for the mapping; wire it in as part of building this entry. **How it plays:** every food/water item now carries its own Hunger/Hydration restore value and (for raw meat and fish) a chance of getting sick. In the Inventory screen, clicking a food or water row eats/drinks one of it, showing its effect (e.g. "+30 food") with risky raw/unpurified items flagged by an orange tag; nothing is consumed if it wouldn't help — you get "You're not hungry"/"You're not thirsty" instead. Eating goes by the item's new food value, not its existing category, so no items had to be recategorized — foraged/fished/hunted food stays Resource-category as before. Sound: `eating.mp3` now lives in `Assets/Audio/SFX` as sfx_eating, playing the first 2.4s of chewing with a fade-out at a level matched to the other effects, replacing rather than stacking with the item-drop sound. First-pass values (Claude Code's proposal, for Mike to judge by feel in Play Mode): berries/apple/pawpaw 6/8/8 food, 3/5/3 water, no risk; chestnut/hickory/walnut 12/15/15 food, no risk; greens and mushrooms 3-6 food, no risk; goat milk 6 food/15 water and eggs 6 food/1 water, no risk; raw meat — venison 30, turkey 22, waterfowl 19, chicken 15, small game 9 — 30% sickness risk; raw fish — bluegill 6, crappie 9, bass 11, catfish 15 — 20% risk; the cooked version of any meat or fish is about 4/3 the raw food value (e.g. cooked venison 40) with no risk. Tested: apples and raw venison raised Hunger by the right amounts, tools aren't edible, and only the eating sound played. **Resolved 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — built and tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (restored and hash-checked after), NOT yet committed or pushed.** Bundled with Cooking and Water Purification above in the same Phase 3 batch. Getting sick from raw meat/fish or bad water now runs through a new Sickness mechanic — see the Health_System.md update below — and three new sound files (Cooking.mp3, Upset stomach.wav, water-pouring-a.wav) are sitting sourced-but-unmapped in `Xtra Sound files/`, so cooking and boiling still just play the generic pickup sound for now; see the Audio_System.md note.

- **Done 2026-09-25 (Claude Code) — see Log. Hunger curve and all decay rates are a first proposal awaiting Mike's Play Mode feel.** ~~**Feature: Hydration and Hunger survival stats.**~~ First of the 11 GDD-required Alpha 0.1 systems that have no code yet (Claude did a doc-vs-code audit and found a gap between what's documented and what's built — see Log). Core_Survival_System.md already fully specs Hydration: 0-100, decreases continuously (influenced by temperature, physical activity, illness, weather), with confirmed effect tiers — 75-100 no penalties, 50-74 minor stamina reduction, 25-49 reduced stamina and reduced work efficiency, 1-24 severe penalties and Health loss begins, 0 rapid Health loss. Hunger is also specced (0-100, represents food energy reserves, decreases slower than Hydration, six food categories) but doesn't yet have Hydration's tiered effect breakdown — Health's doc only lists Starvation as one of its five inputs (alongside Dehydration, Illness, Exposure, Fatigue) without numbers. Requesting: (a) both stats implemented as continuously-decreasing 0-100 values on the player, most likely alongside InventoryManager/PlayerController per the existing Managers pattern; (b) Hydration's five effect tiers wired to stamina/work-efficiency/Health per the doc; (c) a first-pass Hunger effect curve proposed by Claude Code (tier breakpoints and Health-loss-at-zero, mirroring Hydration's shape) since the doc doesn't lock specific numbers there — flag it for Mike to confirm once he can feel it in Play Mode, same propose-then-confirm pattern as the Livestock/Wildlife/Plants stat sheets. Decay rates (how fast either stat drains per real minute / in-game hour) aren't specified anywhere and are Claude Code's call to propose, since nothing in the doc locks a number. **Update 2026-09-25 (Mike):** also wants a HUD meter/gauge for both stats — (d) add a Hunger meter and a Hydration/Thirst meter to the HUD, matching however the existing HUD prefab presents things (check `Prefabs/HUD.prefab` for the established look — the pause screen's Fahrenheit/season/time readout is the closest existing reference point). Exact style (bar, radial gauge, icon-plus-fill) and placement are Claude Code's call; should read clearly at a glance without crowding the existing HUD.

- **Confirmed 2026-09-25 (Mike) — see Log.** ~~**Feature: HUD compass.**~~ Mike wants a directional compass on the HUD. No existing design doc covers this — nothing in Discovery_System.md or elsewhere specs a compass, so this is a new, self-contained ask rather than an implementation of something already designed. Keep it simple for a first pass: a heading strip or simple N-indicator showing the player's current facing direction (tied to camera yaw off `PlayerController.cs`/`Main Camera`), cardinal and intercardinal directions (N/NE/E/SE/S/SW/W/NW) at minimum. That distinction matters because Discovery_System.md's Design Rule ("the map should be learned, not revealed — nothing is known until a DiscoverySite is found") governs the Minimap/World Map system specifically, which is still not implemented and stays out of scope here; a compass showing which way is north doesn't reveal any location info and doesn't conflict with that rule. Visual style (a strip across the top of the HUD, a simple corner widget, etc.) and exact placement are Claude Code's call, matching the existing HUD's look. Both should be gated to Editor/debug builds only and never reachable in a release build. Exact hotkey, skip increment, and multiplier value are Claude Code's call.

- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built and tested, NOT YET committed.** ~~**Feature: Wood Gathering / Tree Chopping.**~~ New Wood_Gathering_System.md (Design Draft) written from Mike's own description: the player should be able to chop down trees with the Axe (already exists as a Tool) to get Sticks, Branches, and Logs, then either build with Logs, burn Logs/Branches as Firewood, or (later) craft hand tools from Sticks. Requesting the buildable-now slice only: (a) a chop-down-tree interaction with the Axe equipped, yielding some mix of Sticks/Branches/Logs per tree — exact interaction (hold vs. repeated hits, timing, whether a felled tree leaves a stump) and yield-per-tree are Claude Code's call, same propose-then-confirm pattern as the rest of this doc set; (b) a separate chop interaction that converts a carried/dropped Log or Branch into Firewood — the same Firewood item Fire Building already produces from deadfall, no new item needed, with Logs yielding more/longer-burning Firewood than Branches (exact numbers Claude Code's call). Three new Materials — `sticks`, `branches`, `logs` — added to Item_Data.md with first-pass weights. Explicitly OUT of scope for this entry, per Wood_Gathering_System.md's own Design Rules: building a primitive shelter from Branches (needs Building_Housing_System.md's Unity implementation, which doesn't exist yet) and crafting hand tools from Sticks+Stone+Cordage (no general crafting system exists — deferred post-Alpha per the 2026-09-25 Decisions_Log.md entry; stays Future until a specific hand tool is actually requested, same as Cordage/Arrows). Two sourced sound files await this: `chopping-wood.wav` for the new chop interactions (sfx_chop_tree / sfx_chop_firewood) and `foraging.wav` for the already-built Foraging harvest action (sfx_foraging, currently using the generic pickup sound) — see Audio_System.md's Chopping / Foraging SFX section. **How it plays:** hold left-click on a tree trunk with the Axe equipped — about one swing every 0.9s, 4 stamina each, "Too tired to swing" if stamina runs out, and hungry/thirsty players work slower via the existing work-efficiency value. A broad hardwood takes about 10 swings to fell, a tall one about 13, a shrub about 3 (bigger trees take more); a HUD progress bar tracks it and looking away doesn't lose progress. The tree tips away slowly then fast, settles with a bounce, and sinks away after a few seconds — hardwoods leave a stump, shrubs leave nothing. Everything lands in a wood pile at the base: a broad hardwood gives about 2 Logs/4 Branches/5 Sticks, a tall hardwood about 3/3/4, a shrub 0/1/3 — amounts scale with tree size. Looking at a pile shows its contents; E takes whatever fits (lightest first, Logs last), leaving the rest behind for another trip ("Left behind: 2 Logs"); piles save with the game and disappear once empty. Firewood: 1 Log makes 5 Firewood (10 hours of burn time), 2 Branches make 1 Firewood — split at a pile by swinging the Axe at it (4 swings per Log, then its Branches), or from Inventory while carrying the Axe via a Split button that works like Cook (~4s/Log, ~2s per pair of Branches, Shift for the whole stack). Felled trees and their piles persist across saves; trees don't grow back yet. Decisions Claude Code made: the Axe is now in the starting kit for new games (F11 also grants one to older saves) since nothing else supplied one; trees are part of the terrain rather than separate objects, so a future terrain regeneration would misalign felled-tree state in existing saves — noted as a risk, not acted on. Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save: felling a broad hardwood (2 Logs/5 Branches/6 Sticks, stump, pile, one fewer terrain tree), taking wood near the carry limit (light wood taken, Logs left behind), splitting both at a pile (2 Logs → 10 Firewood) and from Inventory (1 Log → 5 Firewood, 4 Branches → 2 Firewood with a leftover Branch kept), the foraging sound swap, and a full save/reload round trip (both trees still down, both piles intact); save restored and hash-checked after, project settings file unchanged. One visual-only fix noted but not itself re-verified on screen: split firewood pieces in a pile had been merging into long bars, laid out across the row instead. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built and tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save. Committed and pushed 2026-09-26** as part of commit `0563b9e` (all the game work — Phase 3, Wood Gathering, Health Regeneration, Warmth, the Load meter, and the Wood/Rock Piles, including 8 new sound files via Git LFS), with `08b2926` carrying the matching doc/task-list updates. Claude Code confirmed nothing is left uncommitted and `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Bug/Feature: Health regeneration.**~~ Mike confirmed Health correctly drops from starvation, but never regenerates even once Hunger and Hydration are refilled — Health_System.md's own Recovery section ("Health recovers through: Clean water, Quality food, Adequate sleep, Shelter... Recovery should be gradual") was apparently never actually implemented. Mike's rule, now in Health_System.md's Recovery section: Health should slowly rebuild on its own as long as neither Hunger nor Hydration is empty (0). This stacks with the existing Sickness rule that Health doesn't recover at all while sick (Health_System.md's Illness System, Phase 3) — sick overrides everything else, otherwise Health regens whenever both meters are above zero. **How it plays:** Health regens at 3/hour when both Hunger and Hydration are at 50 or higher ("well-fed"), and 1.5/hour when either is lower but still above 0. Claude Code additionally made regen pause completely whenever anything else is actively costing Health — Severe Hunger, Severe Hydration (Core_Survival_System.md's existing tiers), or the new Warmth Severe tier below — rather than letting regen and drain net out simultaneously. **This is a rule call Claude Code made on its own and flagged for Mike to confirm — not yet decided by Mike.** Along the way, Claude Code found the pre-existing Recovery code only healed when *both* Hunger and Hydration were at 75+, essentially non-functional in practice — fixed to match Mike's actual spec. Also caught and fixed a testing bug where the well-fed rate initially read 2/hour instead of the intended 3/hour, traced to a stale value left in the scene. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `0563b9e` (`08b2926` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub. **Update 2026-09-26 (Mike):** confirmed he agrees with the pause rule for Severe Hunger and Severe Hydration, and wants it to also pause while Sick — which it already does via the existing, separate Illness System rule, so no code change is needed there. The sickness-pause "duration" isn't a separate number; it just ends when the Sickness bout itself ends (8-hour/16-hour-cap timer, already built). Mike still plans to playtest the full Hunger/Hydration/Sickness regen behavior himself before calling this fully confirmed.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Inventory weight HUD meter.**~~ The Inventory screen already shows carried weight against the Encumbered/max-carry thresholds when open (I key), but Mike wanted that visible persistently on the HUD, the same way Hunger and Hydration already got their own meters. Pure exposure of data InventoryManager already tracks (`CarriedWeightKg` vs. `EncumberedWeightKg`/`MaxCarryWeightKg`) — no new mechanic, no design decision needed. **How it plays:** a new "Load" meter sits at the bottom of the HUD's meter stack, below Hunger/Hydration/Warmth, with a tick mark at the Encumbered threshold and a green→amber→red color progression as carried weight climbs toward the max-carry cap. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `0563b9e` (`08b2926` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Warmth attribute + HUD meter.**~~ New survival attribute, Range 0-100, moved out of Core_Survival_System.md's Future Expansion ("Advanced temperature systems") into current scope at Mike's request — full ambient exposure, not just a simple fire-proximity gauge. Spec is in Health_System.md's Exposure System section: decreases from outdoor temperature (existing Season/Weather ranges), precipitation (wet drains faster than dry), and Wind (Weather_System.md's confirmed dual role); increases near a lit campfire using the same proximity FireManager already computes for ambient_campfire_crackle (Audio_System.md) — currently the only warmth source in the game, since neither Building/Housing nor a clothing system exists yet (both explicitly Future for this attribute). **How it plays:** a "feels-like" temperature drives everything — outdoor air temperature, minus up to 15°F for wind chill, minus up to another 12°F when the player is soaked. Above 50°F feels-like, Warmth recovers up to 5/hour; below that it falls up to 12/hour at its worst, at 0.3/hour per degree under 50°F, faster while wet. At a steady 25°F feels-like standing exposed, it takes about 10 real minutes to drop from full to the Moderate tier. A wet/soaked status line appears on the HUD alongside the meter. A lit campfire at full heat adds Warmth at 30/hour within 1.5 m, fading to nothing by 6 m away, and also warms and dries the player as a side effect. The F9 debug key now also warms/dries the player; a new debug key, F10, drops Warmth to 20 for testing the low tiers. Effect tiers, tuned to match Hydration's existing shape: 50-74 (Mild) — Stamina ×0.9; 25-49 (Moderate) — Stamina ×0.7 plus 1 Health/hour lost; 1-24 (Severe) — Stamina ×0.5 plus 4 Health/hour lost plus Movement ×0.75; 0 — 8 Health/hour lost plus Movement ×0.6, sharing Health's collapse risk rather than being a separate death condition. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `0563b9e` (`08b2926` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Primitive Storage (Wood Pile / Rock Pile).**~~ Full spec in Wood_Gathering_System.md's "Primitive Storage" section. Originally scoped as Future, sequenced after Building_Housing_System.md's real storage buildings shipped — Mike confirmed he wants the primitive versions built now, with better storage (Storage Shed, etc.) layered in once Building itself gets its first Unity implementation (still Design-Draft-only, no code yet). Reuses the existing felled-tree wood-pile mechanic Wood Gathering already built — a persistent world pile that shows its contents on look and lets the player take/leave items over multiple trips — made player-placeable via the same "Inventory → Build" pattern as the Campfire, Fish Trap, and traps. Wood Pile holds Sticks/Branches/Logs/Firewood; Rock Pile holds Stone (Item_Data.md's first real use for Stone — nothing in the game produces it yet). **How it plays:** a Wood Pile costs 4 Sticks to build; a Rock Pile is free to place. R stores an item into a pile, E takes from it — the same interaction shape as the felled-tree piles. Neither pile has a capacity limit. Both persist across saves even when completely emptied, staying visible in the world as an empty frame (Wood Pile) or a small ring of base stones (Rock Pile) rather than disappearing, unlike the felled-tree piles which vanish once empty. Stone itself still has no source anywhere in the game — the Rock Pile exists and works, but nothing currently produces Stone to put in it; flagged as a known gap, not something this entry was asked to fix. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `0563b9e` (`08b2926` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Stone Gathering.**~~ New Stone_Gathering_System.md (Design Draft) — Stone already exists as a Material (Item_Data.md, 5.0 kg) and the new Rock Pile (Primitive Storage above) is built to hold it, but nothing produces Stone at all. Mike's own description: "Stone should almost copy the same availability as natural resources found throughout the land. Rocks are lightly scattered everywhere, but more densely located around creeks, rivers and cliff side rock deposits throughout the land." **Confirmed 2026-09-26 (Mike):** the cliff-side rock deposit specifically requires a new craftable Tool, the Stone Pick Axe (Sticks + Cordage + Stone) — the base scatter and water-zone stones stay hand-pickup either way. See Stone_Gathering_System.md's Distribution and new Tools: Stone Pick Axe sections for the full spec. **How it plays:** 250 loose rocks total — 150 scattered lightly across the whole property (about one every 30 m), 90 in a denser band along the creek and pond banks, 10 at the foot of the South Ridge outcrop; each is a hold-E pickup worth 1 Stone (5 kg), no tool needed, gone for good once picked up. The outcrop itself is 8 large, half-buried boulders on South Ridge's south face, 16 m below the ridge top and clear of the cabin site (moved once already, off the new-game spawn point). It only yields to the Stone Pick Axe — hold click to swing like the Axe, every 4 blows breaks off a Stone, holds 40 Stone before it's worked out; without the Pick Axe equipped, looking at it just says "mine it with a Stone Pick Axe." Stone Pick Axe recipe: 2 Sticks, 1 Cordage, 1 Stone — crafted from a new Craft row in the Inventory (Mike's materials, Claude Code's quantities). `pick-axe.wav` plays one of six clean strikes per blow. Tested in Play Mode: picked up a stone, crafted the Pick Axe, mined the outcrop, and confirmed the save round trip (gathered stone and the outcrop's remaining count both persisted). **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Natural Tree Fall (Windthrow).**~~ New "Natural Tree Fall" section in Wood_Gathering_System.md, resolving the open wind/storm question Audio_System.md raised when `tree-fall.wav` was sourced. Mike's own description: "random throughout the year, primarily after or during heavy thunderstorms or long winters with a lot of snow buildup." **How it plays:** standing hardwoods can come down on their own anywhere on the property, falling roughly in the wind's direction; shrubs are never blown down, and nothing falls within 6 m of the player. Behaves exactly like a chopped-down tree — stump, wood pile, and the `tree-fall.wav` crash. First-pass odds: about one tree a month on an ordinary day; about one every 8 storm hours during a Thunderstorm; raised for the 12 hours after a storm; and in late Winter/early Spring, scaling with how much snow fell that Winter, up to about one every day and a half after a very snowy one. Storm/snow state saves with the game. Tested in Play Mode: forced a windthrow, and simulated the ordinary-day odds out to about 1 per 30 days. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Primitive Shovel.**~~ New "Tools: Primitive Shovel" section in Wood_Gathering_System.md, requested for two purposes: (1) removing tree stumps that hardwood felling leaves behind — buildable now, since felled trees/stumps already exist; (2) clearing and leveling ground to build on — cross-referenced into Building_Housing_System.md's new Site Preparation note as Future, since Building/Housing has no Unity implementation at all yet for a cleared site to matter to. Requesting just the stump-removal half be built now. **How it plays:** recipe is 3 Sticks, 1 Cordage, 1 Stone (Claude Code's proposed quantities on Mike's un-quantified materials). Equip it and hold click on a stump; after 6 digs the stump is gone for good and the roots give 1 Firewood. `digging.wav` plays one of 16 clean scrapes per push. Ground clearing/leveling stays Future as scoped. Two bugs found and fixed along the way: stumps had a nearly spherical collider that let aim slide off the rim, now a proper cylinder; and newly felled wood piles were landing close enough to a stump to cover its collision box (this affects Mike's save specifically — the Shovel now finds the stump just behind the pile instead). Tested in Play Mode: crafted the Shovel, dug out a stump, confirmed the fixes. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature/Correction: Metal Cooking Pot (Boiling gate change).**~~ Reverses part of the already-shipped, committed Phase 3 Boiling mechanic (Water_System.md's Boiling section, commit `0563b9e`), which currently gated the Boil button on carrying the Bucket. Mike's own reasoning: a wooden Bucket can't go over a campfire to boil water without burning. **How it plays:** Boiling now requires the new metal cooking pot instead of the Bucket — without one equipped, boiling refuses with "Boiling water needs a Cooking Pot – the wooden Bucket would burn." The Bucket now only carries water. Boiling first pours carried water from the Bucket into the pot, up to 4 L at a time, playing `water-pouring-a.wav`; the pot itself is carried, not placed. **Claude Code's own call, flagged for Mike to confirm — not craftable.** Nothing on the property can produce metal, so the pot is in the new-game starting kit alongside the Bucket; F11 grants one to existing saves that predate it, including Mike's own. Claude Code noted a craftable clay pot (or similar) would be a quick change if Mike would rather it be earnable. Tested in Play Mode: boiling refused without the pot, worked with it. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub. **Update 2026-09-26 (Mike):** confirmed — keep it as a starting-kit item, not craftable; no code change needed. Mike does like the clay-pot idea for later (pots, cups, plates, bowls from clay deposits), logged as a Future note in Water_System.md, not this batch.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Primitive Storage — Water/Food/Dry Goods + Pouch/Bag.**~~ New Primitive_Storage_System.md, extending the same primitive-tier pattern the already-built Wood Pile/Rock Pile established to three more categories Mike named, plus a new Pouch/Bag Tool. **How it plays:** all three containers build from the Inventory and work like the Wood Pile (R to store, E to take). Water Barrel costs 3 Logs, holds up to 40 L of water — R pours carried water in with the pour sound, E fills the player's Buckets, purified water first. Food Cache costs 2 Logs + 4 Branches, holds Food; each stored batch keeps its original acquired day, so storing food won't reset its age once spoilage exists. Storage Bin costs 6 Branches + 6 Sticks, holds everything else that isn't a tool (Cordage, hides, furs, feathers, arrows, etc.). Pouch: 1 Deer Hide + 1 Cordage (Claude Code's proposed quantities on Mike's un-quantified materials) — carried, it adds 10 kg to both the max-carry cap (now 55 kg) and the Encumbered threshold (now 40 kg), and the limits drop back to normal if it leaves the inventory. The Inventory now shows two rows of three for Build and Craft, each button explaining on hover what it needs or why it can't be built there. Tested in Play Mode: built all three containers, poured water in and drew it back into the Bucket, stored/retrieved food with its day intact, and confirmed the save round trip (all containers persisted). **Things Claude Code flagged, not yet acted on:** (1) Cordage has no source anywhere in the game — the Pick Axe, Shovel, and Pouch each take one, using up exactly the 3 Cordage a new game starts with, leaving none for snares or the Fish Trap; (2) Storage's E takes everything that fits at once — a proper transfer screen for the Food Cache/Storage Bin (choosing what to take) would be the natural next step; (3) carried water isn't capped by how many Buckets are held — only matters via direct code (as in Claude Code's own tests), not through normal gameplay paths. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub. **Update 2026-09-26 (Mike):** answered — see the new "Feature: Cordage Sourcing" entry below.
- **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Cordage Sourcing.**~~ Cordage already has a design note (Trapping_System.md, confirmed 2026-09-25) but no real source in the game — only the F12 debug kit and the New Game starting kit's 3 units. That gap is now urgent: the new Stone Pick Axe, Primitive Shovel, and Pouch (Primitive_Storage_System.md) each consume one Cordage to craft, using up all 3 a new game starts with and leaving none for snares or the Fish Trap. Mike's own materials: craftable from tall grasses, cattail stalks, animal tendons, "and other natural methods" (leaves room for more); also purchasable from a store with money. Requesting the natural-crafting side be built now — which raw material(s) yield Cordage and in what quantity are Claude Code's call, propose a first pass. The store-purchase path stays Future: Economy_System.md has no actual store/purchasing mechanic built yet (no currency, no shop UI), so there's nothing to hook a purchase into right now. Note: Animal Tendons doesn't exist as an item yet — if used, it'll need adding to Item_Data.md's Resources table (a Hunting/Trapping byproduct, alongside Small Furs/Hide/Antlers). Cattails already exist as a Foraging resource. Tall grass has no existing item — Claude Code's call whether it needs one or whether foraging near grass/meadow terrain is enough. See Trapping_System.md's Sourcing Cordage note for the full history. **How it plays:** a single Cordage craft button uses whichever fibre the player is carrying — 3 Tall Grass, 2 Cattail, or 1 Sinew. Tall Grass is a brand-new item, placed across open meadow and pasture (90 clumps total) via a new editor-only "Homestead → Place Tall Grass" menu; cutting a clump with E gives 3 Tall Grass and the clump regrows after 4 in-game days, with dry stalks usable in Winter too. Sinew is also new, a field-dressing byproduct — 2 per deer, 1 per turkey (Claude Code's choice over a standalone Animal Tendons item). Cattail is the existing forage item, but its single marsh patch only bears in Spring, so Tall Grass and Sinew are the main day-to-day sources. Tested in Play Mode: cut a grass clump (+3), crafted Cordage from each of the three sources, got a clear "Needs…" message with none, and confirmed the clump regrows after 4 days. **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
 **Done 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built, tested, committed and pushed.** ~~**Feature: Sleep System (Sleeping Bag / Tent / Lean-To).**~~ Full spec in Building_Housing_System.md's new "First Build: Sleep System" section. Right now there is no Sleep mechanic anywhere in the game — Save_Data_Model.md already assumes an auto-save trigger "at Sleep," but nothing produces it, and Building/Housing has zero Unity implementation at any tier. Scoped deliberately small: just the bottom two Comfort tiers (Sleeping Bag, and Tent/Lean-To), not the full six-tier progression — Small Cabin and everything above it (including the stove/fireplace, kitchen, sink, and running water additions already sketched in the same doc) stay Future. **What's being asked:** Tent and Lean-To place from Inventory → Build, the same pattern already proven for the Campfire/Fish Trap/this week's primitive storage containers — Tent is a non-craftable Difficulty_System.md starting-kit/Trading Post item (same status as the metal cooking pot), Lean-To is craftable from local materials (Claude Code's first-pass recipe proposal — likely Branches, already established as the "local materials" source, plus Sticks and/or Cordage). The Sleeping Bag needs no placement — usable directly, anywhere, no Build step. Interacting with a placed Tent/Lean-To, or using the bare Sleeping Bag, triggers Sleep: advances time (to morning, or a chosen duration — Claude Code's call), fires the Sleep auto-save Save_Data_Model.md already expects, and applies a recovery/protection effect scaled by shelter tier. Proposing this effect hook into Health_System.md's existing Warmth and Recovery mechanics rather than invent a new stat — a bare Sleeping Bag gives little or no protection from overnight Warmth drain, a Tent/Lean-To reduces or blocks it (similar in spirit to the existing campfire-proximity warming, but passive). Whether there's also a flat Health/Stamina bonus on waking, and all exact numbers, are Claude Code's first-pass proposal — Mike confirms by feel in Play Mode, same pattern as everything else this week. **How it played out:** the Sleeping Bag is a new starting-kit item, not craftable — click it in the Inventory to sleep right where the player stands, no placement needed, and it won't work while standing in water. The Tent is also starting-kit and not craftable, like the metal cooking pot — placed from Build using the Tent carried; E sleeps in it, R packs it back up. The Lean-To is craftable from 8 Branches, 4 Sticks, and 1 Cordage; E sleeps in it, R takes it down and returns 4 Branches. At night (from 19:00) sleep runs until 06:00; sleeping in the daytime is a 3-hour rest instead. During sleep the screen fades out while Hydration, Hunger, Warmth, Health, campfire fuel, weather, and windthrow all keep running; on waking, Stamina refills and the game auto-saves — the "save at Sleep" trigger Save_Data_Model.md has been expecting all along. An early-waking safeguard fires rather than letting sleep cause real harm: Warmth below 20, Hydration or Hunger at 0, or Health below 15 wakes the player early (e.g. "You wake shivering — too cold to sleep"). Shelter tier works through the existing Warmth and Health-recovery mechanics, not a new stat: a bare Sleeping Bag cuts overnight Warmth loss by 25% (0%/0% rain/wind block, ×1.5 Health recovery while asleep); a Lean-To cuts it by 50% (80%/60% block, ×2 recovery); a Tent cuts it by 60% (100%/90% block, ×2 recovery); a Sleeping Bag used inside a shelter adds its own 25% on top, and a nearby campfire still warms as usual. Claude Code's sample numbers at 32°F over 9 hours, Warmth starting at 90 (Health starting at 60): nothing = 31 Warmth/75 Health; Sleeping Bag alone = 46/95; Lean-To = 62/100; Lean-To + bag = 69/100; Tent = 70/100; Tent + bag = 75/100. **Flagged for Mike to judge by feel:** a full night in a shelter healing 60 Health to 100 may be generous — Claude Code called it an easy number to lower once played. **Confirmed 2026-09-26 (Mike):** a full night's sleep healing that much is good as-is — no change needed. Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save: built a Tent and a Lean-To, slept in the Tent from 21:00 to 06:02 with the auto-save firing, slept on the bag alone and triggered an early cold wake after 1.3 hours, packed both back down, and confirmed a save round trip (cut grass and the placed Tent both persisted). Two visual fixes along the way: the Tent looked like a box from behind, now has a proper triangular gable; the Inventory's right panel was resized (smaller type for long costs) to fit the new third rows of Build/Craft buttons. **Note for Mike:** his current save has no Sleeping Bag or Tent — F11 grants both; new games start with both already. The sleep screen fade didn't come through in Claude Code's screenshots (a capture quirk only — the sleep logic itself ran as described). **Resolved 2026-09-26 (Claude Code) — built, tested, committed and pushed** as part of commit `4145888` (`8b3ea6e` carries the doc updates); `master` matches GitHub.
- **Done 2026-10-02 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built and tested, NOT YET committed.** ~~**Feature: Tool Rack (primitive storage).**~~ New "Tool Storage — Tool Rack" section in Primitive_Storage_System.md, resolving the gap Mike hit playtesting Small Cabin's staged build: he hit the carry-weight limit hauling a full hunting/fishing/survival toolkit and had nowhere to stash the tools he didn't need for the trip, since Storage Bin explicitly excludes Tools. Mike confirmed he wants a separate dedicated container rather than folding Tools into Storage Bin — same category-scoped pattern every other primitive storage already follows. Build cost, capacity, and whether to build a real per-item transfer screen for this one specifically (Storage Bin's already-known "take everything at once" gap) were left as Claude Code's first-pass call. **How it plays:** costs 4 Branches + 4 Sticks, same Inventory → Build pattern as the rest; holds any Tool (Axe, Rifle, Bow, Fishing Rod, Cane Pole, Bucket, Fish Trap, Flint and Steel, Recurve Bow, Hammer, Pouch, and so on — anything of `ItemCategory.Tool`, the same check Storage Bin already uses to exclude them). R stores every carried Tool that isn't currently equipped, E takes back whatever fits — same shape as Wood Pile/Rock Pile/Food Cache/Storage Bin, no capacity cap. Kept the all-or-nothing take for this first pass rather than building a selective-transfer screen: swapping one or two specific tools is the common case here, more so than any other primitive storage, so it's flagged again (not newly) as the natural next step if Mike wants it, rather than built now — a bigger lift than this request asked for, and Mike's own framing left it open to defer. Looks like a standing wooden rack with a peg per kind of Tool stored (up to 6). Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's own save — his actual overloaded toolkit from the playtest report (44.95/45 kg carried): built the rack, stored all seven unequipped Tools at once (the equipped Axe correctly stayed on him), took them all back out, and confirmed a save/load round trip with the rack's contents intact. Save restored and hash-verified after. **Resolved 2026-10-02 (Claude Code) — built and tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (restored and hash-checked after), NOT yet committed or pushed.**
- **Done 2026-10-02 (Claude Code) — see Log. Built and tested, NOT YET committed.** ~~**Feature: per-item transfer screen for primitive storage.**~~ Mike: "i think we need a per-item transfer screen now" — upgrading the deferred "take everything at once" gap flagged on Storage Bin and Tool Rack above from a someday-maybe to a real ask. Proposed and built at the broader scope rather than a one-off: one shared screen for every built storage container (Wood Pile, Rock Pile, Water Barrel, Food Cache, Storage Bin, Tool Rack), since they all already share the same R/E mechanic. **How it plays:** E on any of those six now opens a new screen (a fifth, hotkey-less tab inside the existing Inventory/Journal/Map/Trading Post window) listing what's stored, one row per kind — the Water Barrel breaks out by quality (Purified, then Excellent→Unsafe) instead of one lump total, since picking a quality is the point. Take picks one, Shift-click takes the whole stack, same convention as Cooking's Split and Trading Post's Buy/Sell; a row that doesn't fit (too heavy, or the barrel with no Bucket/Canteen room) shows why instead of just vanishing. A shortcut button up top keeps the old one-press behavior too — "Take Everything," or "Fill Bucket (best quality)" for the barrel — so emptying the whole thing is still one click. Storing (R) stays untouched: bulk, out in the World, since Mike's ask was specifically about picking what comes back out. Also scoped out on purpose: a felled tree's wood pile and a Small Cabin's staged CabinSite keep the old instant take-all on E rather than opening the screen — they're quick, incidental piles visited often while gathering, not the deliberate storage this request was about; flaggable if Mike wants them included too. Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save: built a Storage Bin, Tool Rack and Water Barrel, stored a mix of goods/tools/water into each, opened the screen via the real E interaction on each one, took a single item, Shift-took a whole water-quality stack, used both shortcut buttons, switched the open window from one pile straight to another, closed back to normal play, confirmed R still bulk-stores unchanged, and confirmed a full save/load round trip with every container's contents intact. One real bug caught before shipping: the screen's refresh was double-firing on every take (its own explicit refresh plus a redundant reaction to the inventory-changed event that same take fired), briefly leaving stale duplicate rows for a frame — fixed by dropping the unnecessary subscription, since gameplay is fully paused while the screen's open anyway so nothing else can touch the pile. Save restored and hash-verified after. **Resolved 2026-10-02 (Claude Code) — built and tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (restored and hash-checked after), NOT yet committed or pushed.**

## 05_Documentation/Livestock

Status: Done (3/3 Alpha 0.1 species confirmed)

Purpose (confirmed 2026-09-22): per-species data sheets (concrete stats — feed consumption, milk yield, breeding cooldown, and similar) for each livestock animal. Distinct from Game_Systems/Livestock_System.md, which covers the general mechanic and is done.

Docs added and confirmed 2026-09-22 by Claude, via a propose-then-confirm pattern (Claude drafts real-world-grounded numbers, Mike confirms or adjusts before they're locked):

- Goats.md — Slow breeder (Spring-only, 1 kid/cycle, 30-day maturation), high-value ongoing milk. Missed milking reduces yield rather than spoiling it.
- Chickens.md — Mid-speed maturation (20 days), unconditional daily eggs (not breeding-gated, unlike goat milk), halved egg output in Winter, non-seasonal hatching. Culling a hen for meat has no downside beyond the lost future eggs.
- Rabbits.md — Fastest breeder of the three by explicit design direction (15-day maturation, ~20-day full breeding cycle, 4 kits/litter), no ongoing daily resource between litters. Shares Trapping_System.md's "Small Furs" resource rather than a separate one. Population is intentionally uncapped — regulated by Livestock_System.md's existing feed-shortage pressure, not a hard limit.

Design principle established 2026-09-22 (Mike): each livestock type has a clear, non-overlapping advantage/drawback rather than similar stats with different names.

Owner: Claude

---

## 05_Documentation/Plants

Status: Done (5/5 Foraging categories confirmed)

Purpose (confirmed 2026-09-22): per-species data sheets for foraged/grown plants (identification, nutritional value, seasonal windows, and similar), distinct from Game_Systems/Foraging_System.md's general mechanic.

Docs added and confirmed 2026-09-22 by Claude, grouped by Foraging_System.md's own category structure:

- Berries.md (Blackberries, Raspberries) — confirmed identical, 3 units/patch, annual regrowth
- Fruit.md (Wild Apples, Pawpaw) — confirmed; Pawpaw has a shorter shelf life/worse Preservation outcome than Wild Apples, the one deliberate differentiator in this category
- Nuts.md (Hickory Nuts, Walnuts, Chestnuts) — confirmed; Hickory/Walnut identical, Chestnuts the one shorter-shelf-life exception; Nuts still benefit from active Preservation (drying/storage) on top of a long natural shelf life, not exempt from it
- Edible_Plants.md (Dandelion, Wild Onion, Cattail) — confirmed; fast Spring regrowth (not annual); Cattail tied to Water_System.md's discovered water sources, same pattern as Waterfowl
- Mushrooms.md — confirmed; named real species (Morel, Dryad's Saddle, Oyster Mushroom) rather than staying generic. Morel is the rare, annual, ground-growing prize (and the intended seed for the future poisonous-species system via its real False Morel lookalike); Dryad's Saddle and Oyster Mushroom are common, fast-regrowing, wood-growing, and low-risk

Owner: Claude

---

## 05_Documentation/Wildlife

Status: Done (3/3 Wildlife categories confirmed)

Purpose (confirmed 2026-09-22): per-species data sheets (concrete stats) for wildlife, distinct from Game_Systems/Wildlife_System.md, which covers the general ecosystem design and is done.

Docs added and confirmed 2026-09-22 by Claude, grouped by Wildlife_System.md's own category structure:

- Small_Game.md (Rabbit, Squirrel) — confirmed identical (1 Meat + 1 Small Furs each); Squirrel not gated behind nut-grove discovery, kept simple for Alpha 0.1
- Medium_Game.md (Turkey, Waterfowl) — confirmed differentiated: Turkey is Dawn-only, 4 Meat + Feathers; Waterfowl has its own all-day-but-wetland-gated timing, 3 Meat + Feathers, and migrates (absent in Winter) per Season_System.md's existing note
- Large_Game.md (White-Tailed Deer) — confirmed; 8 Meat; Hide and Antlers are year-round with a higher chance during a confirmed Fall "Rut Window" (second half of Fall, all-day activity). Hunting_System.md's "Antlers (seasonal)" wording was updated to match.

Owner: Claude

## 05_Documentation/Fishing

Status: Done (4/4 Alpha 0.1 species confirmed)

Purpose (confirmed 2026-09-22): per-species data sheets for the four Fishing_System.md species, distinct from that doc's general mechanic — one file per species, matching the Livestock pattern rather than Wildlife/Plants' category grouping, since Fishing_System.md already treats each species individually.

Docs added and confirmed 2026-09-22 by Claude, via the same propose-then-confirm pattern as Livestock/Wildlife/Plants, grounded in a new Fish Species Behavior Grounding section added to Research.md:

- Bluegill.md — easiest/most reliable catch (common, active all day, works with the cheapest gear), smallest yield (1 Food/catch)
- Crappie.md — normally the hardest to find (deep structure, dawn/dusk/night only), 2 Food/catch, but gets a real Spring Spawn Window where schools move shallow and become dramatically easier to catch — modeled as an actual behavior shift, not just a label, the same way the Deer Rut Window works
- Bass.md — 3 Food/catch, effectively locked out of the passive Fish Trap method (sight hunter, needs active lure presentation) — the one species that specifically rewards Rod and Reel / Cane Pole over passive infrastructure
- Catfish.md — 4 Food/catch (top of the four-species scale), no hard time-of-day gating, and especially effective in Fish Traps (scent-driven bottom scavenger) — the mirror image of Bass

Fishing_System.md's Fish Species and Fish Traps sections were updated with cross-references to each new file. Feature_Backlog.md's "Fishing per-species stat sheets" awareness-only entry was removed now that the gap is closed.

Owner: Claude

2026-09-23 — Claude Code — Implemented Audio_System.md's Alpha 0.1 spec. Mixer: new `Assets/Audio/Homestead.mixer` with Music / Ambient / SFX / UI groups under Master, each with an exposed volume (MasterVolume, MusicVolume, AmbientVolume, SfxVolume, UiVolume); every AudioManager/PlayerAudio source routes through its group, and the player's per-channel volume (PlayerPrefs, now including a UI channel) is applied through those parameters. All 22 files wired per the Sourced Files table (`SFX/footsteps rain.wav` left unwired): menu theme on the main menu, day/night music by TimeManager daylight; ambient Day (dawn–dusk, non-precipitating weather, scaled Summer 100% / Spring 85% / Fall 70% / Winter 40%), Night, Light Rain, Heavy Rain (also Thunderstorm), Snow, and Wind (always on, scaled by WeatherManager.WindStrength); a positional Water loop at each discovered Water Source (audible within 25 m); footsteps by surface with pitch per movement state, sprint breathing, and encumbered breathing while moving at ≥30 kg (new `PlayerAudio` on the Player prefab); item pickup/drop on InventoryManager's player inventory (new ItemsAdded/ItemsRemoved events — moving items into storage counts as a drop); discovery chime, journal-updated (timed with the HUD's "Journal Updated" line), and milestone fanfare — when a discovery is also a first-of-category milestone, only the fanfare plays rather than both at once; UI click/back on MainMenu and PauseMenu (menu opening clicks, closing via Resume or Esc plays back). **Prerequisite added, as the doc anticipated:** no surface-tag system existed, so a minimal `GroundSurface` component (Grass/Dirt/Gravel) now marks ground colliders; the placeholder ground is Grass and unmarked ground defaults to Grass — real terrain will need its texture layers mapped to surfaces. **Levels:** the files vary widely in loudness (footsteps/sprint breathing ≈ −50 dB RMS, menu theme ≈ −14 dB), and AudioSource volume can only attenuate, so each group has a fixed gain (Ambient +14 dB, SFX +19 dB, UI +6 dB) with per-clip volumes set from measured loudness to role targets — a starting mix from measurements, not listening; tune by ear in the Inspector. Music/ambient import as Streaming. **Two sourcing issues for Mike:** (1) `Ambient/Day.wav` and `Music/Gameplay Day.wav` are byte-for-byte the same file, so daytime plays the same recording as both ambience and music; (2) `UI/menu button click.wav` and `UI/menu cancel click.wav` are also identical, so click and back sound the same. Both are wired as documented; replacing either file fixes it with no code change. Verified in Play Mode (mixer params at runtime, routing, music/ambient across season/time/weather, discovery/milestone/journal cues, water loop, pickup/drop, footsteps/breathing states incl. gravel, pause-menu sounds). 2026-09-23 — Claude Code — Fixed the silent discovery audio. Answers to the three checks: (1) AudioManager *is* subscribed to `DiscoveryManager.Discovered` and `MilestoneReached` (in Start); (2) all three clips *are* assigned on it in Bootstrap (discovery bell ding, page turn, success fanfare); (3) both branches *do* call PlayOneShot — the first-of-category branch plays the fanfare only, by design. Root cause was one level down: every UI/system cue plays through a single `uiSource` via `PlayOneShot`, which multiplies by the source's own volume, and `CreateSource` starts all sources at volume 0. Every other source sets its volume explicitly (SFX voices per play, music/ambient/player loops per frame), which is why footsteps, pickup/drop, ambience and music worked; `uiSource` never did, so it was silent. That also means the menu click/back sounds use the same silent path — worth re-listening to them after this fix. Fix: `uiSource.volume = 1` after creation, so each cue's own volume and the UI mixer group set the level. My earlier Play Mode test only checked `isPlaying`, which is true even at volume 0 — that gap is why this slipped through. Verified after the fix: uiSource at volume 1 on the UI group; the fanfare fires for a first-of-category discovery at spawn, the page turn when the HUD shows "Journal Updated", and the chime for non-first discoveries. Mike's save was backed up and restored byte-for-byte.

2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Built World.unity's real terrain per Property_Layout.md, replacing the flat 200m placeholder ground. It's generated by a new editor script, `Assets/Scripts/Editor/PropertyTerrainBuilder.cs` (menu: Homestead → Build Property Terrain). The script is deterministic and rerunnable, so layout changes go in that script rather than being hand-painted (a rebuild would overwrite hand edits). Result: a 480m square property with gently rolling ground. South Ridge is the clear high point (~30m at the cabin site, next-highest ground elsewhere ~26.6m). Spring Hollow's pool feeds a 319m creek running south-west into Bass Hole's pond, with water levels only ever dropping downstream and at least ~1m of bank above the water everywhere. About 68% of the land is forest (8,852 placeholder trees: two low-poly hardwoods with trunk colliders plus a walk-through understory shrub), which thickens over the outer 30m instead of a wall. Open pasture sits around the cabin site and up to the Old Fence Line Trail, plus a few glades (north-east, an old field south-east, creek bottomland, pond and spring clearings). Texture layers are Grass (97%), Dirt (the fence-line trail plus worn paths from the cabin to each site) and Gravel (creek, pond and spring banks). `GroundSurface` now maps terrain layers to footstep surfaces, so footsteps follow the painted ground automatically. Sites moved onto the new ground: South Ridge Cabin Site (18, -68), Spring Hollow (-13, 109), Old Fence Line Trail (-7, 92) on the pasture's north tree line, Briar Patch (190, -5) on the pasture's east forest edge, and Bass Hole (-161, -140) on the pond's shore. Bass Hole is the farthest from the cabin (193m vs. the spring's 186m). Player spawn is at (20, -40), facing up the ridge toward the cabin site. Also: loading a save now lifts the player onto the ground if their saved position is underground, so pre-terrain saves stay playable. Verified in Play Mode with Mike's slot1 backed up first and restored byte-for-byte afterwards: his save loaded him from the old y=0.05 onto the terrain surface, grounded; footstep surface read Dirt on the trail, Gravel at Bass Hole and Grass on the pasture; walking into Bass Hole discovered it at its new position; tree trunks block movement; New Game spawns grounded; ~80-100 fps in the editor. Known limits, not fixed: (1) The cabin site is no longer spotted from the spawn point by sight, because the ridge slope blocks a level gaze, so it's found by walking up the slope. (2) Discovery records in existing saves keep the old flat-ground site positions (these only matter once a map exists). (3) Water is an opaque placeholder with no swimming, so walking into the pond puts the camera underwater. (4) The first build's HDRP terrain setup rewrote the HDRP package's cached DefaultHDTerrainMaterial.mat under Library/ (untracked). The builder now uses its own copy, and Unity's package cache can re-extract the original if it matters. Added a Git LFS rule for `*TerrainData.asset` (binary, rewritten on every rebuild). Not committed yet.

2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Investigated the "rain ambience during clear, sunny weather" bug. The audio was correct and the visuals were wrong. It was the bug entry's cause (2), but it lives in the rendering rather than WeatherManager. Mike's save at the time of the report had WeatherManager in Heavy Rain (`rainedToday: true`, mid-morning), and AudioManager was correctly playing the heavy-rain loop. Switching weather to Clear in Play Mode faded rain out and Day ambience in, as Audio_System.md specifies. The sky looked sunny because weather had no visible effect. DayNightCycle dimmed the sun (to 20% in heavy rain), but the scene's automatic histogram exposure brightened the image straight back up, and there were no clouds or weather fog. Fix in `DayNightCycle.cs`: a runtime-only weather volume (created in Play Mode, so no profile asset is modified) sets three things per weather type, blending over in-game time like the sun dimming already did. (a) Fog distance, per Weather_System.md's visibility effects: Clear 400m, Cloudy 300m, Light Rain 200m, Heavy Rain 120m, Thunderstorm 80m, Cold Front 350m, Snow 150m, Wind 400m. (b) Cloud cover, from none when clear to fully overcast in rain, storms and snow, using HDRP's Cloud Layer. (c) Exposure compensation, so 70% of the sun's weather dimming actually shows on screen. Also moved the fog's base height from 0m (tuned for the old flat ground) to 20m, because the new terrain sits 15–35m up where the old fog had mostly faded. All values are Inspector-tunable on Day Night Cycle. Verified with before/after captures from the same spot: heavy rain is now a dark, overcast, hazy day, and clear weather looks as it did before. Mike's slot1 was backed up and restored byte-for-byte after every Play Mode run. Stopping Play Mode auto-saves over the slot, and one run's auto-save did overwrite it before the restore. Not addressed: there are still no rain or snow particles. Weather_System.md doesn't call for them, so they'd be a new feature for Claude/Mike to decide on. Not committed yet.
