2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Added visible precipitation per Weather_System.md's new Visual Feedback section. The cloud/fog/exposure fix this entry wanted bundled was already committed and pushed (`368bf07`). New `Precipitation.cs` component on a "Precipitation" object in World.unity. It builds two Unity particle systems (rain and snow) that spawn in a 50m square box 15m above the camera and are simulated in world space, so drops don't slide along with the player. The Visual Effect Graph package isn't installed, so this uses the built-in particle system with two new HDRP transparent unlit materials and generated textures in `Assets/Art/Weather/`. Rain falls as streaks at 10 m/s: 1,500 drops/s for Light Rain, 6,000/s shared by Heavy Rain and Thunderstorm. Snow is 800 flakes/s, falling at 1.2 m/s with a wandering drift. Clear, Cloudy, Cold Front and Wind have none. Both slant with WeatherManager's wind direction and strength, build up or die away over 8 seconds when the weather changes (frozen while paused), and dim with daylight so they don't glow at night. Rates, speeds, area and drift are all Inspector-tunable. It's presentation only and never changes weather state. Verified in Play Mode with Mike's slot1 backed up and restored byte-for-byte. With Heavy Rain forced, dense rain streaks fell over the pasture (~11k drops at steady state, ~80 fps in the editor). Switching to Snow faded the rain out completely and brought in drifting flakes (12k at steady state, ~73 fps). Known limit: particles pass through tree canopies, so it rains under the forest just as hard as in the open. Not committed yet — flagged an uncommitted Weather_System.md edit from Claude to fold into this commit.

2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Implemented all four new weather/UI entries.

(1) **Pause screen °F:** `PauseMenuController.cs` now shows `WeatherManager.TemperatureF` (e.g. "Cloudy, 55°F"). WeatherManager still works in °C internally, per the Units note.

(2) **Overhead cover:** new `OverheadCover.cs` helper, general rather than tree-specific. Anything solid overhead, found by an upward raycast that ignores the terrain collider, counts as full cover, so future buildings work automatically. Tree crowns count as partial cover. Their colliders are trunks only, so crowns are read from the terrain's tree instances: prototypes taller than 4m, with crown radius taken from the mesh bounds. `Precipitation.cs` checks 5 points around the camera 4 times a second and eases over 1s. Under a full crown, rain/snow emission drops to 30% (Inspector: Canopy Shelter 0.7). Under a roof, emission stops and drops already in the air are hidden immediately. Verified in Play Mode: pasture 6,000 drops/s, under a hardwood crown 1,800/s, and under a temporary test box 0 with particles hidden. The test box was removed.

(3) **Lightning and thunder:** new `Lightning.cs` on the World scene's weather object (renamed from "Precipitation" to "Weather Effects"). During a Thunderstorm a strike comes every 8–30s. 80% are distant (1.5–4km): a 6,000-lux flash from low on the horizon and a dull rumble (low-pass down to ~600Hz, quieter) after a 4–12s delay. 20% are close (150–500m): a 50,000-lux flash, a visible jagged bolt (new `Lightning Bolt.mat`, bright unlit so it reads through storm fog), and a sharp, unfiltered crack under 1.5s behind. Each flash flickers 2–3 pulses. Thunder is delayed at the speed of sound (343 m/s) and routed to the Ambient mixer group. **Audio note:** `Thunderstorm.wav` turned out to be a 4½-minute storm recording (rain bed plus many thunderclaps), not a single thunder one-shot. Loudness analysis found clear thunder events 15–24 dB above the rain bed, so AudioManager now holds five hand-picked slices (7s, 45.8s, 92.8s, 198.8s, 232.2s; 8–12s each). Each strike plays a different slice with a fade-out, never the same one twice in a row, which gives more variety than the single clip expected. Import changed to Compressed In Memory / Vorbis / 48kHz (it was 96kHz, and decompressing it in memory would take ~100MB) so slices can seek. Claude Code can't listen: slices were picked from loudness and low-frequency energy, and Mike should confirm by ear that they sound like thunder rather than rain. Verified in Play Mode: a forced close strike placed the bolt ~283m out, thunder started ~0.8s later on the Ambient group with the filter open, and a capture showed the flash lighting the pasture with the bolt above the tree line. Audio_System.md's sfx_thunder row still says "not yet wired" and can be updated.

(4) **Ground snow:** new `GroundSnow.cs` (same object). It paints an extra snow terrain layer onto a runtime copy of the terrain data, so the terrain asset is never modified (verified: still 3 layers, not dirty, after a snowy Play Mode run). Repainting is spread over 16 frames of 64 rows to avoid hitches. Buildup is +15% cover per in-game hour while snowing at or below 0°C (full cover in ~7 in-game hours). Snow holds while it stays below freezing and melts at 2%/hour plus 1%/hour per °C above freezing, so +5°C clears full cover in ~14 in-game hours. All rates are Inspector-tunable. Cover uses the same OverheadCover logic: 30% of the open-ground snow under a full crown, none under a roof. The amount is saved in world.json ("groundSnow") so snow survives a reload; older saves without it start at none. Footsteps keep the surface under the snow: `GroundSurface` now ignores terrain layers it has no mapping for (there's no snow footstep sound). Verified in Play Mode at −5°C: buildup matched the rate exactly (0.29 h → 4.4%), full cover painted white over the pasture with 30% under canopy, and at +5°C it melted at the expected rate. 2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Fixed both of Mike's playtest audio bugs.

(1) **Footstep cadence:** confirmed the cause by measuring the three recordings. Grass has a footfall every 0.31s (3.2/s), dirt every 0.545s (1.8/s), and gravel irregularly, around 0.7s. Looping them played each surface at its own recorded pace. `PlayerAudio.cs` no longer loops them. It plays **one footfall per stride of distance actually covered**: 1.1m walking, 1.6m sprinting, 0.65m crouching, so about 2.3, 3.1 and 2 steps a second at PlayerController's speeds, identical on every surface. Each step plays a single footfall cut from the surface's recording (start times measured by onset detection and stored in AudioManager as `footstepsGrassTimes`/`DirtTimes`/`GravelTimes`), picked at random without repeating back-to-back, with ±5% pitch variation and a 0.3s play-then-fade. Crouching is still quieter. The old walk/sprint/crouch *pitch* settings are replaced by stride lengths, all Inspector-tunable. Verified in Play Mode by holding W through the Input System: grass footfalls played from the measured start times while walking at 2.5 m/s, and switched to dirt on the Old Fence Line Trail and back to grass off it. Mike should check by ear that the cut footfalls sound clean (Claude Code can't listen), especially gravel, whose recording was the least regular. The start times can be nudged in AudioManager's Inspector.

(2) **Ambience under cover:** the Light Rain, Heavy Rain and Snow ambient layers now have a "Soften Under Cover" flag. AudioManager checks the listener's cover with the same `OverheadCover` signal Precipitation uses (4×/s, easing over 1s) and scales those layers to 75% under a full tree crown and 40% under a roof (Inspector: Canopy/Roof Ambient Volume). That's "a little softer" under trees, with a roof still letting some rain on the roof be heard. Other layers (day/night, wind) are unaffected. Verified in Play Mode during Heavy Rain: open 0.220 → under a crown 0.165 → under a temporary test roof 0.088, wind unchanged. The test object was removed.

2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Added the debug-only time controls. **Hotkeys (in-game, while playing):** F5 skip 1 day; F6 skip to the start of the next season; F8 skip straight to the start of Winter; F7 cycle fast-forward ×1 → ×10 → ×60 (a day in 30s) → ×360 (a day in 5s) → ×1. A yellow on-screen readout shows the speed and date while fast-forwarding, and briefly after each skip. The same skips (1 day, 7 days, next season, or a specific season) are also in the Unity menu under **Homestead → Debug Time**, enabled only in Play Mode. How it works: `TimeManager.cs` gained `DebugSpeed` (multiplies only its own clock; Time.timeScale, movement and physics are untouched, and pausing still stops it), `DebugSkipDays` and `DebugSkipToSeason` (keeps the time of day, and goes to next year's if already in or past that season). Skips run through the normal `AdvanceMinutes`, so every hour/day/season event fires on the way and weather/temperature keep up. Fast-forward resets to ×1 on returning to the menu. **Release safety:** everything is inside `#if UNITY_EDITOR || DEVELOPMENT_BUILD`, `TimeDebugControls` creates itself at startup instead of living in a scene, and nothing is saved. Verified by compiling the scripts as a non-development Windows player: the release Assembly-CSharp.dll contains no TimeDebugControls, DebugSpeed or DebugSkipDays. Verified in Play Mode with real key presses through the Input System: F5 went Spring 3 09:06 → Spring 4 09:06, F8 went → Winter 1 (weather re-rolled, 41°F), and F7 ×60 advanced 15.6 in-game hours in 19.4 real seconds (a day per 30s) with timeScale still 1. Returning to the menu reset the speed to ×1. Known limit: ground snow only sees the weather at the end of a skip, not the snowfall during skipped days, so after F8 the ground starts bare and builds from there. Fast-forward (×60/×360) shows buildup and melt properly. Mike's slot1 was backed up and restored byte-for-byte. Not committed yet.

Drafted a new `Game_Systems/Maple_Sugaring.md` (16th Game_Systems doc): sap season keyed to the same 32°F freeze-thaw signal Weather_System.md's precipitation rule uses rather than a fixed date; tappable trees are a subset of the existing generic hardwood forest (first system needing Maple identified specifically within it — Claude Code's call how); new Tap/Spile tool and Sap Bucket collection, sap accumulating over time and spoiling if left too long (Trapping_System.md-style check-in pattern); processing raw Sap into Maple Syrup over the existing Fire System, meaningfully lossy per the real ~40:1 ratio (simplified exact number left to Claude Code) so Syrup stays low-yield/high-value; new Raw Sap and Maple Syrup items, not yet added to Item_Data.md since this stays out of active scope for now. 2026-09-24 — Claude Code — Implemented all three new entries.

(1) **Precipitation by temperature:** `WeatherManager.cs` now converts Light Rain and Heavy Rain to Snow whenever the temperature is at or below 0°C/32°F, in any season. It checks when weather is rolled, every hour as the temperature moves, and on load. Mike's current save had exactly this bug (Light Rain at −5.4°C on Winter 5), and loading it now resolves to Snow. The conversion is one-way, as the doc specifies, and Snow's gameplay state applies. A Thunderstorm below freezing stays a Thunderstorm (lightning, thunder and storm lighting continue) but a new `PrecipitationWeather` reports Snow ("thundersnow"). Precipitation particles, the rain/snow ambience and ground snow read that, so a freezing storm shows snow particles and plays snow ambience, not rain. New `IsSnowing`; `IsRaining` now excludes thundersnow. Verified in Play Mode at 17°F: forced Heavy Rain → Snow, and forced Thunderstorm → still Thunderstorm, with snow falling at 777/s, Snow ambience (no rain) and lightning still scheduled.

(2) **Fall foliage** and (3) **tree-top snow:** built together. `PropertyTerrainBuilder.cs` now generates trees with branches under the crowns (so winter trees are bare limbs, not poles), snow caps on every upward face of crowns and branches, and a random per-face value (new `Foliage Mask.png`) that the leaf and snow materials alpha-clip against. Raising the cutoff removes leaves, or adds snow patches, one face at a time. Each tree prefab carries a new `SeasonalFoliage` component with its colours. The terrain was rebuilt from the same seed: identical 8,852 tree positions and crown shapes. New `SeasonalTrees.cs` (on Weather Effects) swaps the terrain's tree prototypes for play-time copies with their own materials, so no asset is modified. It drives:
- colour: green → Fall colour over Fall 0–55% (red-orange broad hardwoods, golden tall ones, red shrubs), browning slightly late in Fall;
- leaf drop: over Fall 45–100%, so Winter is bare;
- leaf-out: pale green returning over Spring 0–45%;
- crown and branch snow: tracks the ground-snow amount, and crown snow only sits on leaves still on the tree.
`GroundSnow.cs` became `SeasonalGround.cs` (same script GUID and save key, so scenes and saves carry over). It also paints **leaf litter** under tree crowns as leaves drop through late Fall, keeps it through Winter (under the snow) and fades it over early Spring. New `RuntimeTerrain.cs` provides the single play-time terrain-data copy both components share.

Verified in Play Mode by jumping the date and capturing the same forest edge: Summer unchanged; Fall 12 in full red/orange/gold; Fall 23 with crowns thinning face by face and shrubs almost bare; Winter 7 with bare branched trees over snow-covered ground; Spring 7 with leaves half back and litter fading (33% under the player). Steady editor frame rate ~77 fps (was ~80). Each ground repaint step measured ~11ms per 64 rows, so it's now 32 rows (~5ms) per frame to avoid hitches when snow changes.

Known limits: snow on the thin branches is subtle at a distance. Litter and bare trees don't change OverheadCover, so a bare winter forest still shelters snowfall like a leafy one. All seasonal changes are colour/visibility of the placeholder low-poly trees, so a real art pass will want proper foliage. Mike's slot1 was backed up and restored byte-for-byte. Not committed yet.

2026-09-25 — Claude Code — Implemented Hydration and Hunger, plus Health, which both of them feed into. **What's in:** a new `SurvivalManager` on Bootstrap's Managers object, following the existing manager pattern. Health, Hydration and Hunger each run 0-100, are saved in `player.json` under a new "survival" section, and reset to full on New Game. Older saves have no survival section, so they load with full stats. Hydration and Hunger drain continuously while playing. They pause with the game, and the debug fast-forward and day skips deliberately don't drain them, so weather testing doesn't starve the player. Sleep, when it's built, should call `SurvivalManager.PassHours` for the time slept. **Hydration effects, per the doc's tiers:** stamina ×1 / ×0.85 / ×0.65 / ×0.4 / ×0.4 for 75-100 / 50-74 / 25-49 / 1-24 / 0. Work efficiency ×1 / ×1 / ×0.75 / ×0.5 / ×0.4, exposed as `WorkEfficiency` for chopping, harvesting and building once those exist. Health loss is 1.5 per in-game hour at 1-24 and 8 per hour at 0. The stamina multiplier lowers both the stamina ceiling and its refill rate in `PlayerController`. **Proposed for Mike to confirm by feel — none of these are locked by any doc:** (1) Hunger uses Hydration's same five breakpoints but milder: stamina ×1 / ×0.9 / ×0.75 / ×0.5 / ×0.5, work ×1 / ×1 / ×0.85 / ×0.6 / ×0.5, Health −1/hr at 1-24 and −4/hr at 0. When both stats are low, the worse stamina and efficiency value applies (they don't multiply together), but the Health losses do add up. (2) Drain rates: Hydration 2.8/in-game hour, so full to empty takes about 36 in-game hours (45 real minutes) standing still. Hunger drains at half that, about 72 in-game hours (90 real minutes), per "hunger decreases slower than hydration". Walking multiplies drain by ×1.2 for Hydration and ×1.15 for Hunger. Sprinting multiplies it by ×2 and ×1.6. Heat speeds Hydration drain, ramping from 80°F to ×1.5 at 95°F+. Cold speeds Hunger drain, ramping from 40°F to ×1.3 at 10°F and below. Illness has a hook (`IllnessHydrationMultiplier`) waiting for the Illness system. (3) Health slowly recovers, +2 per in-game hour, while both stats are 75 or above and nothing is draining it. The doc doesn't say whether Health regenerates, and without recovery any loss would be permanent. (4) Health reaching 0 raises a `Collapsed` event and logs a warning, but nothing else happens yet, since the doc says death is TBD. **HUD:** new meters in the bottom-left corner of `HUD.prefab`, matching the discovery popup's dark-green panel and cream text: Hydration (blue) and Hunger (amber) bars, with Health (red) above them. The Health bar only fades in while Health is below full. A meter below 25 turns red and pulses. **Drinking, added so the stats can be refilled:** looking at the creek, spring or pond within reach shows "[Hold E] Drink", which restores 20 Hydration per drink. The water surface now has a collider on the Water layer so it can be looked at. The player's CharacterController ignores that layer, so you still wade in: tested standing on the creek bed under the surface. `PropertyTerrainBuilder` sets this up too, so a terrain rebuild keeps it. Water quality and illness risk aren't modelled, so every source is safe for now. I also added the on-screen interaction prompt, which didn't exist before, so item pickups now show "[Hold E] Pick up …" as well. **Eating isn't implemented:** there's no inventory UI and no food or cooking system yet, so Hunger can only be refilled with the debug key for now. That needs its own entry. **Bug fixed along the way:** looking steeply down, the interaction raycast hit the player's own capsule, which would have blocked both drinking and picking up items at your feet. It now skips the player. **Debug keys:** F9 refills all three stats, and F10 drops Hydration and Hunger to 20 to show the warnings and Health loss. Both are compiled out of release builds, like the time controls. **Verified in Play Mode** by continuing Mike's save: stats drain, every tier gives the expected stamina and efficiency values, Health loss and recovery rates match, collapse fires at 0, and the survival section saves and loads. The stamina ceiling showed 85 at Hydration 55. Drink raised Hydration 48.7 → 68.7. The HUD screenshot looked right. While testing, Hydration rose twice by 20-40 without my input. Mike confirmed he was pressing E in the Game view at the time, so those were real drinks, not a bug. Mike's slot1 was backed up and restored byte-for-byte after each run. Not committed yet.

2026-09-25 — Claude Code — Implemented the Minimap with fog of war. **Look:** a round, north-up minimap in the top-right of the HUD, 250 px across and showing 160 m of ground, centred on the player. The player is a cream arrow that turns with your facing, and an "N" sits on the rim. It uses the same dark-green panel and cream rim as the rest of the HUD. **Terrain layer:** chosen as a pre-baked image rather than a live camera, painted from the terrain data when World loads (about 0.4 s, during the loading screen): ground colour from the terrain layers (grass, dirt trails, gravel), hill shading, faint 4 m contour lines, darker tree canopy from every placed tree, and the creek, spring and pond from the water mesh. It reads like a field map and doesn't go dark at night or change with weather. It doesn't change with the seasons either (no autumn colour or snow); that could come later if wanted. **Fog of war (Discovery_System.md's new subsection):** the whole map starts black. Ground within 30 m of where you've actually walked is revealed permanently, fading out over another 10 m for a soft edge, and never re-fogs. It's tracked on a 2 m grid by a new `MapManager` on Bootstrap's Managers. It's saved in `discovery.json` under a new "map" section (gzipped, about 2 KB for a well-explored map) and cleared on New Game. Older saves start fully black and reveal from wherever you're standing. The 30 m radius is a first proposal, roughly how far you take in the land through the woods. It doesn't grow with Knowledge Progression yet; that's a clean follow-up if Mike wants it. **Marker layer:** exactly the doc's rule. A site's icon appears only once that `DiscoverySite` is discovered, and only from `DiscoveryManager`'s records, never from the scene, so walking past the spring reveals the ground but not the 💧 until it's found. 2026-09-25 — Claude Code — Implemented the three game screens: **World Map (M), Inventory (I) and Journal (J)**. **Shared behaviour:** they're one window with a tab per screen. A screen's key opens it, switches to it from another screen, or closes it if it's already showing; Esc or the X also closes. The tabs can be clicked too. While a screen is open, the game enters a new `GameState.Menu`, which I chose over leaving the game running. The in-game clock, survival drain, fog reveal and the player all stop, time scale is 0, and the cursor is freed. Unlike the pause menu, world audio keeps playing and the pause menu doesn't appear. Saving, discoveries and audio treat Menu as still in-game, so quitting or returning to the main menu from a screen still saves. Screens can't open over the pause menu or in menus, and the HUD hides while a screen is open. M, I and J are new rebindable actions in `InputSystem_Actions` (the gamepad Select button also opens the Map). Letter keys are ignored while you're typing a journal note, so typing "j" doesn't close the journal. Esc while typing belongs to the text field. Everything is built in code (a new `UiKit` helper) in the HUD's dark-green and cream look, so the World scene only gains one "Game Screens" canvas. **World Map ("Property Map"):** the whole 480 m property, north up, using exactly the minimap's data: the same fog of war and the same terrain image (now shared through a cache rather than generated twice), and markers only for discovered sites. Markers are labelled with the site's name. You're shown as an arrow turning with your facing, over a softly pulsing halo so you stand out even on top of a marker. Beside the map are a "Discovered" list (icon, name, category) and "You've walked N% of the property". **Inventory:** carried items grouped by category, with quantity and total weight, plus a Carrying panel showing kg and lb against the 45 kg cap. A weight bar has a tick at the 30 kg Encumbered line and turns amber when encumbered and red at the cap, and a status line explains the effect. Clicking a carried tool equips it and clicking it again unequips it, with the equipped tool highlighted and named; other items aren't clickable. Storage transfer is left out, per the entry. There's still no Eat/Use, since no food system exists; the rows are ready for a Use action when one does. **Journal ("Field Journal"):** section tabs down the left for all seven sections, each with its entry count and a dot if anything is unread. The middle column lists the section's entries newest first, with the in-game date. The right shows the selected entry and marks it read. Discovery, observation and milestone entries are read-only. Notes has a "+ New Note" button, and a player note opens as editable title and body fields that save when you click away, switch entry or close the journal. Delete takes two clicks ("Click again to delete"). **Verified in Play Mode** on Mike's current save (his 08:45 playtest, 26% explored, 5 sites found): I opened the Inventory with a simulated I key, and the state went to Menu with the clock stopped, time scale 0 and the cursor freed. I added test tools, and equip, unequip and plain-item rows behaved as described; the carry cap stopped items that didn't fit. J switched to the Journal; selecting Spring Hollow showed its facts and cleared its unread dot. I created, edited and saved a note, pressed J while typing (the journal stayed open), and deleted it with two clicks. The World Map showed Mike's real explored area, all five labelled markers and the player halo. Esc (`TogglePause`) closed back to Playing with the cursor locked and the clock running, and opening a screen while paused was refused. Partway through, simulated keys stopped reaching actions because the Unity Editor itself wasn't the focused app; the project routes keyboard input to actions only while the Game view has focus. So I opened the Map by calling the same method the M key calls. That's a test-harness limit, not a bug: I and J had already worked through real key events. **Save note:** Mike's slot1 had been rewritten by his own 08:45 playtest after my earlier backup, so I took a fresh backup of that save before testing and restored it byte-for-byte after each run, waiting for the quit-save to finish first. Not committed yet.

2026-09-25 — Claude Code — Implemented **Fire Building** and **Water Collection** only, per Mike; the rest of the batch (Foraging, Cooking, Water Purification, Eating) is untouched until he's playtested these two. **How to play it:** a New Game now starts you carrying **Flint and Steel** and a **Bucket**. That's a first-proposal starting kit: Water_System.md says buckets are "available from Day One", and something has to light the first fire. For an existing save, press **F11** (debug builds only), which gives Flint and Steel, a Bucket and 6 Firewood. Firewood comes from **40 deadfall piles** (fallen branches) scattered through the woods: look at one and hold E to pick up 2-3 Firewood. **Building a fire:** open the Inventory (I). Under **Build → Campfire (3 Firewood)**, press Build and it appears about 1.8 m in front of you. The button explains itself when it can't build: not enough Firewood, standing in water, ground too steep, or too close to another fire. **Tending it:** look at the campfire and one context prompt does whatever it needs next. "Light Fire (6.0 h of fuel)" needs Flint and Steel carried, not equipped. "Add Firewood (burning, 8.0 h of fuel)" adds 2 hours per piece, up to 12. Otherwise it reports its state, e.g. "Burning (…, full)", or "Burnt out — add Firewood to rebuild it" once the logs are gone. A lit fire burns down in real time at the normal clock rate: 1 Firewood = 2 in-game hours = 2.5 real minutes, and the 3 used to build it give 6 hours. It dies down visibly in its last hour, then goes out, leaving ash in the stone ring. Like Hydration and Hunger, the debug fast-forward and day skips don't burn fuel; Sleep should call `FireManager.PassHours`. Fires save and load (world.json, "fires"), including lit state and remaining fuel, and New Game clears them. **Look and sound:** a ring of stones, a tepee of logs, layered glowing flames, rising embers and a flickering warm light (45 cd, about 12 m reach), which only lights while burning. There's a looping crackle routed through the Ambient volume. **The crackle is a placeholder synthesised in code.** No fire recording exists in the project, so a real campfire loop should be sourced like the other audio. Drop it on the Campfire prefab's AudioSource and it replaces the placeholder automatically. `FireManager.NearestLit` is ready for Cooking and Water Purification to use. Fire gives no warmth yet, since there's no body-temperature system. **Water Collection:** with the Bucket **equipped** (Inventory → click it), looking at water shows "Fill Bucket (Excellent water, 10 L)" instead of Drink. One hold fills it. Water is carried as 1 L units at 1 kg each, so a full 10 L bucket adds 10 kg, matching the doc's "physically demanding". Each bucket carried holds 10 L, shared across qualities, and filling respects the 45 kg cap. Once the bucket is full, or when it isn't equipped, the same water offers "Drink" as before. **Water keeps its source's quality,** as separate items: Water (Excellent) / (Good) / (Questionable) / (Unsafe). The one water surface is now zoned: within 14 m of the spring is **Excellent**, the creek is **Good**, and the creek-fed pond is **Questionable**. I judged it slow, warm water rather than stagnant, so not Unsafe; that's easy to change. `PropertyTerrainBuilder` sets the zones on rebuild, and there's a menu item, Homestead → Apply Water Quality. **Drinking carried water:** in the Inventory, clicking a water row drinks 1 L (+20 Hydration, the same as drinking at the source). It's refused when you're not thirsty, so none is wasted. Every quality is still safe to drink; illness risk and boiling belong to the Water Purification entry, which can use the quality already tagged on each item. **New items** (for Claude to add to Item_Data.md): `firewood` (Material, 1.5 kg), `flint_and_steel` (Tool, 0.2 kg), and `water_excellent` / `water_good` / `water_questionable` / `water_unsafe` (Consumable, 1.0 kg per litre, non-perishable). The existing generic `water` item is untouched and could become Purified Water. **Verified in Play Mode** on Mike's current save: the spring filled 10 L of Excellent (+10 kg), and the prompt then switched to Drink. Two 1 L drinks from the Inventory raised Hydration 49 → 69 → 89. The creek topped the bucket up with exactly 2 L of Good. The pond reads Questionable. Build was refused in the spring ("Can't build a fire in water") and succeeded on the cabin-site pasture: 3 Firewood → 6 h unlit. Light Fire lit it (flames, light and crackle on), and Add Firewood took it to 8 h. Passing 10 hours burned it out (logs gone, ash, light off), and adding a Firewood and relighting worked. A save → main menu → Continue brought the fire back in place, still lit, with its fuel, and the water and equipped bucket persisted. A deadfall pile gave 2 Firewood. New Game started with exactly Flint and Steel and a Bucket and no fires. I tuned the fire's look by screenshot at night: the first attempt drew as cyan squares (additive blending ignores alpha under HDRP), and I switched to premultiplied blending with HDR emission. Mike's slot1 was backed up and restored byte-for-byte, after waiting for the quit-save. Earlier I stopped a World-only Play Mode session Mike had left idle, with his OK; nothing is saved in that mode. Not committed yet.

2026-09-25 — Claude Code — Fire Building cleanup batch, all three items done and verified in Play Mode. **Extinguish and relight:** a new second interaction key, **R** (gamepad right shoulder), is a hold like E, so a stray tap can't douse the fire. It's a new rebindable `Player/InteractAlt` action and shows as a second prompt line. On a burning campfire the prompt reads "[E] Add Firewood (burning, …)" and "[R] Put Out Fire (keeps the Firewood left)". Putting it out keeps every hour of remaining fuel and leaves the logs in the ring. For the feel I chose a short burn-down: the flames stop at once, and the light and crackle fade over 1.5 s rather than snapping off. Relighting is the usual "[E] Light Fire (7.1 h of fuel)" with Flint and Steel. On a fire that's out but still has wood, R offers "Add Firewood", so E isn't the only option there. The mechanism is a small reusable `ISecondaryInteractable` interface, available for any future object with two actions. **campfire.wav → ambient_campfire_crackle:** moved to `Assets/Audio/Ambient/Campfire.wav` and put on the Campfire prefab's AudioSource, so the synthesised crackle is now only a fallback. The source is a 141 s, 96 kHz, 32-bit float stereo WAV (108 MB). It's imported as mono so it positions in 3D, compressed at 44.1 kHz and held compressed in memory (about 1 MB at runtime, better than streaming with several fires). The original stays in LFS. It plays at volume 0.5 (a new Crackle Volume setting on the prefab), dropping as the fire dies down, and still follows the Ambient slider. **drink.mp3 → sfx_drink:** moved to `Assets/Audio/SFX/drink.mp3` and added as a new `SoundCue.Drink` on AudioManager, at volume 0.24 to match the measured loudness of the pickup and drop sounds. It plays for both drinking at a source and clicking water in the Inventory. Fixed along the way: drinking carried water also triggered AudioManager's generic item-drop sound (any item leaving the inventory plays it), so consuming an item now plays its own sound instead (`AudioManager.PlayOnConsume`). That's ready for eating.mp3 later. Building a campfire was also playing the drop sound twice, and now plays it once. `eating.mp3` is left in `Xtra Sound files/` as asked. **Verified** on Mike's 12:01 save, which had a lit campfire with 7.2 h of fuel: both prompt lines showed. Put Out Fire kept the fuel at exactly 7.05 h; after the fade the light and crackle were off and the flames had stopped, with the logs still there. Relighting with E brought everything back with the same 7.05 h. The prefab's AudioSource played "Campfire" at 0.5. Drinking from the Inventory played only "drink" (no drop sound), and drinking at the spring played "drink" too; both gave +20. Mike's slot1 had changed since my last backup (his 12:01 playtest), so I took a fresh backup of that save first and restored it byte-for-byte afterwards. Not committed yet.

Sickness: an 8-in-game-hour bout (~10 real minutes) at stamina ×0.6, Hydration draining 1.75× faster, 1.5 Health/hour lost with no regen, stacking to a 16-hour cap, shown as a pulsing red HUD line with hours remaining and a cause message; saves with the game, and F9 now also cures it. Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (restored and hash-checked after): correct Hunger gains from apples/venison, only the eating sound playing, Sickness cutting stamina and Health as specced and surviving a save/reload, cooking working only at a lit fire (Shift-cooking both bluegill at once), boiling Good water into Purified Water, and 400 drinks of Good water producing sickness ~4% of the time (target 5%). **Kickoff message, ready to send:**

> Five items queued from the last few days of design work:
>
> **1. Stone Gathering + Stone Pick Axe.** New Stone_Gathering_System.md. Stone already exists as a Material (5.0 kg) but has no source in the game. Two ways to get it: (a) loose stone — a light, even scatter of hand-pickup rocks (hold E, no tool, like the deadfall piles) across the whole property, denser along the creek/Bass Hole banks; (b) a new cliff-side rock deposit on South Ridge (added to Property_Layout.md), which is a real rock deposit rather than loose fieldstone — this one requires a new craftable Tool, the Stone Pick Axe (Sticks + Cordage + Stone), equipped to gather from it. Exact rock counts/spacing, dense-zone density, pick-up yield, the deposit's exact look, and the Pick Axe's craft interaction/exact material quantities are all your call — propose a first pass, I'll confirm by feel in Play Mode, same as everything else so far. Two sounds are ready whenever this is built: `pick-axe.wav` and `digging.wav` are both already in `Xtra Sound files/` (digging.wav is for the Shovel below).
>
> **2. Natural Tree Fall (Windthrow).** New "Natural Tree Fall" section in Wood_Gathering_System.md. Standing trees should be able to fall on their own from weather, not just from chopping — random year-round, weighted way up during/after a Thunderstorm or after a long, snowy Winter (both now cross-referenced in Weather_System.md), otherwise rare. A windthrow tree should behave exactly like a chopped one: same Sticks/Branches/Logs yield into a wood pile, same stump, same `tree-fall.wav` crash sound (hardwoods only). Exact odds and how much each trigger weights the chance are your call.
>
> **3. Primitive Shovel.** New "Tools: Primitive Shovel" section in Wood_Gathering_System.md — two purposes. First, removing tree stumps that hardwood felling leaves behind (this is buildable now, since stumps already exist). Second, clearing/leveling ground to build on, cross-referenced into Building_Housing_System.md's new Site Preparation note — that half is future, since Building/Housing has no implementation yet for a cleared site to matter to, so just note it there for later. For now, please just build the stump-removal half. I didn't specify materials for this one — Claude (this doc) proposed Sticks + Cordage + Stone as a first pass, same as the Pick Axe, but that's not locked, so use your judgment or flag if you think something else fits better. The `digging.wav` sound above covers this too.
>
> **4. Metal Cooking Pot (Boiling correction).** This one reverses something already shipped: Water_System.md's Boiling mechanic currently gates the Boil button on carrying the Bucket (part of Phase 3, commit `0563b9e`), but a wooden Bucket can't actually go over a campfire without burning. Please re-gate Boiling on a new craftable Tool, a metal cooking pot, instead — the Bucket's role narrows to transporting water and other liquids only, everywhere else unchanged. `water-pouring-a.wav` is sourced and ready for the pour-into-pot interaction (sfx_pour_water in Audio_System.md). Craft materials, capacity, and whether the pot is placed or carried are your call.
>
> **5. Primitive Storage — Water/Food/Dry Goods + Pouch/Bag.** New Primitive_Storage_System.md, same pattern as the Wood Pile/Rock Pile you already built: a Water Barrel (filled by pouring the Bucket into it), a Food Cache, and a general Storage Bin for everything else, plus a new Pouch or Bag Tool for carrying non-liquid resources to them (parallel to how the Bucket carries water). I didn't specify build costs, capacities, or the Pouch/Bag's materials — Claude (this doc) proposed Deer Hide + Cordage for the Pouch/Bag as a first pass, not locked. Build cost/capacity/deposit interaction for each container are your call, same as the Wood Pile/Rock Pile.
>
> One more thing, not a build ask: I'm going to confirm the `Upset stomach.wav`/`vomiting.wav` Sickness wiring myself through my own playtest and let you know — no need to answer that one.
>
> One consolidated report when it's all done, same as usual.

2026-09-26 — Claude Code — Reported all five items built and tested. **Stone Gathering + Pick Axe:** 250 loose rocks (150 scattered ~1/30m across the property, 90 in a denser band along creek/pond banks, 10 at the outcrop's foot), each a hold-E pickup worth 1 Stone (5 kg), gone for good once picked up, placed via a new editor-only "Homestead → Place Stone" menu. South Ridge outcrop is 8 large half-buried boulders on the ridge's south face, 16m below the top, moved once already off the new-game spawn point; only yields to the Stone Pick Axe (hold click, every 4 blows breaks off a Stone, 40-Stone capacity before worked out), un-mineable by hand ("mine it with a Stone Pick Axe"). Pick Axe recipe: 2 Sticks, 1 Cordage, 1 Stone, crafted from a new Craft row in Inventory. `pick-axe.wav` plays one of six strikes per blow. **Windthrow:** hardwoods fall on their own anywhere on the property (never within 6m of the player, never shrubs), same stump/pile/`tree-fall.wav` as a chopped tree. First-pass odds: ~1/month ordinary day, ~1/8 storm-hours during Thunderstorm, raised for 12 hours after, and scaling with accumulated Winter snow up to ~1/1.5 days after a very snowy Winter. Ground clearing stays Future as scoped. Fixed two bugs found in testing: stumps had a near-spherical collider (aim slid off), now a proper cylinder; and newly felled piles were landing close enough to cover a stump's collision box — affects Mike's own save specifically, now finds the stump just behind the pile. **Metal Cooking Pot:** Boiling now refuses without it ("Boiling water needs a Cooking Pot – the wooden Bucket would burn"); Bucket narrowed to water-only. Boiling pours the Bucket into the pot first, up to 4L at a time, with `water-pouring-a.wav`; pot is carried, not placed. **Claude Code's own call, flagged for confirmation:** the pot isn't craftable — nothing on the property produces metal, so it's in the new-game starting kit like the Bucket, with F11 granting one to existing saves (including Mike's); offered a craftable clay-pot alternative as a quick change if Mike prefers. **Primitive Storage:** Water Barrel (3 Logs, holds 40L, R pours Bucket in, E fills Buckets purified-first), Food Cache (2 Logs + 4 Branches, holds Food, preserves each batch's original acquired day), Storage Bin (6 Branches + 6 Sticks, holds everything else non-tool). Pouch: 1 Deer Hide + 1 Cordage (proposed quantities), carried, adds 10kg to both the max-carry cap (55kg) and Encumbered threshold (40kg), drops back if unequipped. Inventory now shows two rows of three for Build/Craft with hover explanations. **Flagged, not acted on:** (1) Cordage has no source anywhere in the game — the Pick Axe, Shovel, and Pouch each need one, using up exactly the 3 a new game starts with, leaving none for snares/Fish Trap; (2) Storage's E takes everything at once — a real transfer screen would be the natural next step for the Food Cache/Storage Bin; (3) carried water isn't capped by Bucket count except through direct code (as in testing), not normal gameplay. Sleep runs 19:00-06:00 at night, or a 3-hour rest by day; Hydration/Hunger/Warmth/Health/campfire fuel/weather/windthrow all keep running through it; waking refills Stamina and fires the auto-save Save_Data_Model.md has been expecting. An early-wake safeguard fires on Warmth <20, Hydration or Hunger at 0, or Health <15. Shelter tier hooks into the existing Warmth/Health-recovery mechanics rather than a new stat: Sleeping Bag alone cuts Warmth loss 25% (×1.5 Health recovery), Lean-To 50% (×2), Tent 60% (×2), with rain/wind block percentages for each and a Sleeping Bag stacking +25% inside a shelter. Sample numbers at 32°F/9 hours: Warmth/Health end at 31/75 with nothing up to 75/100 with Tent+bag — Claude Code flagged the 60-to-100 full-Health-recovery number as possibly generous. Tested in Play Mode: built both shelters, slept a full night in the Tent with auto-save firing, triggered an early cold wake on the bag alone at 1.3 hours, confirmed pack-down/pack-up and a save round trip; fixed the Tent's back-side visual and resized the Inventory panel for the new third button rows. **Cordage Sourcing:** one Craft button uses 3 Tall Grass, 2 Cattail, or 1 Sinew. 2026-09-27 — Claude Code — Built and tested Small Cabin, Building/Housing's first real Unity implementation. New `clay` item (3.5 kg, Material). Small Cabin is a new `PileKind.Cabin` in WoodManager, same Inventory → Build pattern as everything else, plus three things new to this one: the Hammer must be equipped to build it (`CanBuildPile`, the same "equip the tool for the job" pattern as the Axe/Pick Axe/Shovel), a bigger clear footprint checked automatically against both other piles and standing trees/un-dug stumps (Site Preparation's "automatic check" option), and no take-down — it's permanent. The Hammer's own recipe (2 Sticks, 1 Cordage, 1 Log, as this doc set already proposed) is craftable now too, bundled in as promised. Clay comes from the Primitive Shovel dug at a creek/pond bank (checked against the existing water-surface raycast in a ring around the aim point — no placed deposit, so it never runs out, unlike the South Ridge outcrop). The stove/fireplace furnishing is a real `FireManager` campfire (new `BuildFurnished` call), unlit and unfuelled until tended — since Warmth-by-proximity and Cooking already key off any lit campfire by distance alone, this needed zero changes to either system, confirmed live (lit the hearth, Warmth rose standing next to it, Cooking recognized it in reach). Sleeping in the cabin (`Shelter`/`SleepManager`) is the best tier yet: 75% Warmth-loss cut, blocks all rain and wind, ×2.5 Health recovery, one step above the Tent. **One real design correction found only by testing in Play Mode:** the first quantities proposed (20 Logs/10 Branches/15 Tall Grass/12 Stone/6 Clay) totalled over 250 kg — many times the player's 45 kg carry limit — because `BuildPile` needs the whole cost carried at once, same as every other pile, and Logs/Stone are heavy per unit (8 kg / 5 kg). Cut down to 2 Logs, 4 Branches, 15 Tall Grass, 2 Stone, 1 Clay (35 kg total, tested actually building it). Also hit and fixed a Unity gotcha along the way: WoodManager lives directly in Bootstrap.unity, so a `[SerializeField]` default value that's already been baked into the scene (by an earlier forced asset refresh) doesn't update just by changing the C# literal — the scene's saved values needed fixing too. Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (backed up and restored byte-for-byte after, per the playtest-save-protection habit): built a cabin, confirmed the hearth spawned and could be lit, warmed the player and was recognized for cooking, slept in the cabin, confirmed the Hammer-equipped gate and Clay's bank-detection ring both behave correctly at the boundary. Not yet committed or pushed. Docs updated (Building_Housing_System.md, Item_Data.md — also caught and fixed the Hammer's weight, listed as 2.0 kg but the actual shipped item is 1.2 kg).


2026-09-27 — Mike — The 35 kg recipe builds, but it's the wrong fix: it made a first permanent residence cost less to build than a Lean-To. His call — gathering for the cabin should take real time and multiple trips, not fit in a single 45 kg haul. Directed a mechanic change: place a build site, then deposit materials into it across multiple trips, the same accumulate-over-time pattern the Wood Pile already uses for felled-tree yield. Three specifics confirmed: visible as it accumulates (not an invisible counter), freely interactable (add to or take back at any point, nothing locked in early), and the Hammer gates completion once the site's fully stocked, not placement. This puts the original, more substantial quantities back on the table.

2026-09-27 — Claude Code — Rebuilt Small Cabin's construction as a staged deposit, per Mike's direction, rather than patching the weight problem with smaller numbers again. New `PileKind.CabinSite`: placed free from the Inventory screen (no Hammer, no cost), same footprint/site-prep checks as before. It's a `WoodPile`, not a `Shelter` — ordinary storing and taking (R/E), just capped per material at what Small Cabin still needs (`WoodPileState.Accepts` + new `WoodManager.CabinRequired`), so depositing more than required is refused rather than wasted, and a new `BuildCabinSite` visual grows the pile as logs, branches, grass, stone and clay stack up in their own corners. The Inventory screen's Small Cabin button now does double duty: places a site normally, or — once a nearby site is fully stocked and the Hammer's equipped (`WoodManager.CanCompleteCabin`) — completes it instead (`CompleteCabin`), converting the site in place into the same working Cabin as before (Shelter view, hearth via `FireManager.BuildFurnished`, sleepable). Quantities restored to the original 20 Logs/10 Branches/15 Tall Grass/12 Stone/6 Clay, now that nothing needs to fit in one carry. **One real gap this revision also caught:** the first pass never actually added Cabin/CabinSite to `WoodManager.Buildable`, so no Inventory button ever existed for it — invisible before because that pass's Play Mode testing called `BuildPile` directly rather than through the UI. Tested in Play Mode on a copy of Mike's save (backed up/restored byte-for-byte again): placed a site with no Hammer or materials carried at all (confirmed free), deposited 40 Tall Grass against a 15 requirement and confirmed exactly 15 went in with the other 25 staying with the player, confirmed the Complete button stays unavailable both understocked and (once stocked) without the Hammer, hauled the full recipe across several trips (Logs alone took four, at 8 kg each), completed it, and confirmed the resulting cabin still sleeps, lights, warms and cooks exactly as the first pass verified. Also re-ran the existing Wood Pile and Water Barrel storing paths as a regression check, since the per-material cap touched shared code — both still worked, barrel's shared-litre cap included. Not yet committed or pushed. Docs updated (Building_Housing_System.md) to record the mechanic change and its reasoning.

## 2026-10-03 — General "Drop Items" capability (gap found + resolved)

**Gap found (Mike), playtesting:** "i think we need to be sable to drop items if we are carrying too much or just don't need them." Confirmed via project search this didn't already exist anywhere — the only offload paths were the primitive storage piles (Wood Pile, Rock Pile, Water Barrel, Food Cache, Storage Bin, Tool Rack), each requiring that specific container and only accepting its own item category. No general "shed weight anywhere" action existed.

**Resolved (Mike), asked directly:** dropped items stay in the world as real pickup objects where dropped, rather than vanishing — retrievable later. Documented in Systems/Inventory_System.md under a new "Dropping Items" section, cross-referenced from Game_Systems/Primitive_Storage_System.md.

First-pass scope proposed (not yet built): drop from the Inventory screen (I), single item or whole stack (same split as the shared transfer screen), appears a short distance in front of the player using the existing ground-pickup interaction, no decay/despawn by default. Equipped-Tool drop-while-equipped vs. must-unequip-first left to Claude Code's judgment.

Hand-off drafted for Mike to relay to Claude Code.


## 2026-10-03 — Drop Items: build reconciled (Claude Code report)

Claude Code built the Drop action (Inventory screen, click/Shift-click per-item/stack, ~1.2 m toss, water refused, real `ItemPickup` pending real models, no despawn timer, equipped Tool drops directly). Added beyond the proposal: world-persisted dropped piles (`dropped` block in `world.json` via new `DroppedItems` manager), perishables keep their original acquisition day instead of refreshing, and same-item drops merge into one pile.

Tested in Play Mode via direct method calls (not real UI clicks yet) — single drop, 20-arrow merge, equipped-Axe drop, full save/restore round trip, weight returned to 19.0 kg. Mike's slot1 save backed up and restored byte-for-byte.

Committed code only as `1de876f`. A pile-overlap nudge (keeps different-item piles from stacking on top of each other) was added after that commit, compiles clean, but has **not** been run in Play Mode yet.

Docs reconciled into Systems/Inventory_System.md's "Dropping Items" section. Open items for next check-in: confirm the overlap fix once tested, and confirm the real Inventory UI (not direct calls) behaves as expected once Mike clicks through it himself.


## 2026-10-03 — Hammer: Dismantle/Remove built structures (gap found + resolved)

**Gap found (Mike):** "can we make the hammer remove or dismantle, built things too?" Confirmed via re-read of Building_Housing_System.md: the Hammer only builds/completes today. Only Lean-To (partial Branches refund) and Tent (re-pack, not consumed) can currently be taken down; Small Cabin explicitly has no take-down, and no primitive storage pile/Tool Rack/build site can be removed once placed.

**Resolved (Mike), asked directly (scope):** dismantle covers everything, including permanent housing — not just the primitive/undoable tier. Confirmed as a deliberate, flagged reversal of the doc's existing "buildings are rarely replaced" / "older structures remain useful" philosophy for any structure the player actively chooses to remove.

## 2026-10-03 — Tool Hotkeys: build reconciled (Claude Code report)

Claude Code built the 10-slot hotbar (keys 1-9, 0). Assign via a "Key –" cycle button on each Tool row in Inventory, or point-and-press a number directly. Pressing an assigned key equips/toggles off that Tool; a dropped/stored/lost Tool's key shows "Not carrying the Axe" but keeps its assignment; empty slots show an assign-from-Inventory hint. Only active during live gameplay (not while Inventory/other screens open or paused). Slot data saves with the inventory block; old saves load with empty slots. Sleeping Bag/Tent excluded (not equippable Tools). No input-binding conflict with the project's existing 1/2 = Previous/Next bindings — read directly instead of adding new input actions.

Tested in Play Mode via the Inventory UI (assign, move, equip, toggle, not-carried, empty slot, save/reload, old-save compatibility). **Not yet tested: real physical key presses** — Unity ignores simulated keyboard input when unfocused, so only the underlying logic was exercised. Also worth a glance: new "Key –" button layout and hint-text overflow. Mike's slot1 save backed up/restored byte-for-byte. Committed as `2fb66d2`.

Docs reconciled into Systems/Inventory_System.md's "Tool Hotkeys" section. Open items for next check-in: confirm real key presses work, and glance at the new UI for layout/overflow issues.

Confirmed via project search: no portable light source exists anywhere (only the fixed Campfire/Small Cabin hearth), and Core_Survival_System.md already lists "Lighting" as one of Fire's functions with nothing fulfilling it away from a fire.

**Resolved (Mike):** add both, staged by what the project can support now. Torch and Lantern are buildable-now proposals (primitive materials, Core_Survival_System.md's new "Portable Lighting: Torch, Lantern, Flashlight" section). Flashlight is Future, deferred until a battery item exists.

Mike's own framing scopes this as research to have ready, not a build request yet. Researched real-world off-grid sizing (sources below) and wrote a new doc, **Power_System.md**, covering: load tiers mapped to the existing Housing progression (Large Cabin ~1-2 kWh/day via its conditional water-pump "equipment," Cottage/Workshop ~6-8, Farmhouse ~15-20), the sizing formulas (daily Wh, battery Ah via days-of-autonomy/depth-of-discharge, solar panel wattage via peak-sun-hours, wind turbine output via capacity factor, generator sizing to peak load, inverter sizing to peak load), and all three generation options tied to the Utility Structures already named in Building_Housing_System.md (Well House/Windmill/Solar Shed) plus a new Gas Generator option. Weather-dependent output (Solar/Wind) ties to Weather_System.md's existing weather/wind state. Cross-referenced from Building_Housing_System.md (Utility Structures, Large Cabin's "equipment" line) and Item_Data.md (Flashlight's battery dependency).

Sources used: howtogosolar.org (off-grid homestead solar guide), offgridbenchmark.com (cabin power setup examples), voltcalcs.com (load-calculation formulas), attainablehome.com (small wind turbine output by rating).

Item_Data.md updated with first-pass Torch and Lantern rows (weights/recipes unconfirmed, Claude Code's call).

Hand-off drafted for Mike to relay to Claude Code — Torch/Lantern only; Power_System.md is research to have on hand, not a build task.


## 2026-10-03 — Torch & Lantern: build reconciled (Claude Code report)

Claude Code built both. Torch: 1 Sticks + 1 Cordage, needs Flint and Steel (uncon­sumed) to light, burns ~3 in-game hours (~4 real min), consumed/auto-unequipped when out, keeps remaining time across snuff/relight, dims in its last 24 in-game minutes. Lantern: 2 Clay + 1 Cordage (Claude Code's own material choice), burns a new `lamp_oil` consumable ($3/bottle at Trading Post, 8h/bottle, 16h tank = 2 bottles), Refill button on the Lamp Oil row, lights with no Flint and Steel needed (flagged — say so if Mike wants it to require flint too), brighter/wider pool than Torch, never consumed itself. Only the equipped light can be lit; switching/stowing snuffs it. HUD status line + Inventory hours-left readout. Both save state across reload. New items: torch (0.5kg), lantern (1.0kg), lamp_oil (0.5kg/bottle). Trading Post buyback: Torch $1, Lantern $4.

Tested in Play Mode: crafting, lighting with/without flint, snuff/relight, torch burnout, refill + capacity cap, save/restore, night screenshots. **Not tested: real physical mouse clicks** (Unity ignores simulated input unfocused, same caveat pattern as Tool Hotkeys' untested key presses). Two incidental Inventory UI layout bugs found and fixed (Craft row overlapping "Esc to close" hint at 12 recipes; Lantern oil readout clipped). Light position corrected (was floating as a mid-air orb, moved to lower-right view). Mike's slot1 save backed up/restored throughout.

**Flagged, unresolved:** no weather/sleep interaction (rain doesn't douse Torch, lights don't burn during sleep), no held/world models yet, a dropped half-burnt Torch loses its remaining time, and a note about "two Lanterns stacking" was cut off in the report — worth asking Claude Code to finish that thought next check-in.

## 2026-10-04 — Player Collapse decided: "wake weakened"

Mike answered the one open design fork from the gap check: when Health (or, via Health drain, Warmth) hits 0, the player collapses and wakes weakened. They are not killed and the save does not end. Recorded in Health_System.md under "0 — Player Collapse" with a first-pass spec, labeled "(Claude, not yet confirmed)": fade to black, 8 in-game hours pass with the world still running, wake at the nearest owned sleeping spot (otherwise where you fell), Health 20, Hunger and Hydration floored at 25 so a collapse can't loop, Warmth floored at 40, wet and Sickness cleared, a 4-hour Weakened state (stamina ×0.7) with a HUD line, a cause-naming message, no item loss in the first pass. Updated the roadmap's Open Questions entry from "open" to "decided, designed, not built". Not added to Needs Claude Code: per the GDD Development Rule, build it after or alongside the Winter Preparation playthrough. Claude Code should correct the first-pass specifics if they don't fit the existing code. Uncommitted, along with the roadmap rewrite and the gap-check entry above.

## 2026-10-07 — Player Collapse: no distance cap; repeated collapses cost progressively more

Mike answered the two open items from the last entry: the wake shelter has no distance cap ("No"), and repeated collapses should cost more, progressively ("yes, progressively"). Recorded in Health_System.md. Mike chose the direction only, so the numbers are a first pass labeled "(Claude, not yet confirmed)": a saved count of recent collapses that falls by 1 for every 3 collapse-free in-game days; 1st collapse = 8h skip / 4h Weakened at stamina ×0.7 / Health 20; 2nd = 12h / 6h at ×0.6 / Health 15; 3rd = 16h / 8h at ×0.5 / Health 10; 4th and later = 16h / 12h at ×0.5 / Health 10 plus one carried item dropped where you fell. Waking floors (Hunger, Hydration, Warmth 50 or higher) don't scale, so there's no unbreakable loop. The message states the recent count from the 2nd collapse on. Also reworded the "No item loss" bullet to match. Claude Code should correct the numbers if they clash with the code; Mike tunes by feel. Still not a build task (Winter Preparation playthrough first). Uncommitted, along with the 10-07 corrections above.

## 2026-10-07 — Playtest (Pioneer, new game): basic resources too scarce, Warmth drops too fast, start season looks wrong

Mike started a new game from the beginning on Pioneer ("No axe to start with") and reported: difficulty finding sticks or branches for a campfire; rocks, sticks, branches and tall grass should be more readily available; the game started in winter and Warmth dropped rapidly; wants body temperature to rise through exertion, "just like if we are getting a workout."

Findings from the docs (not the code): (1) The only source of Sticks and Branches is felling a tree with an Axe; deadfall gives Firewood only. Pioneer has no Axe, and the Primitive Axe needs 2 Sticks, so Pioneer can't get started. That breaks Difficulty_System.md Design Rule 2. (2) Stone already has a scatter (150 base, 90 creek, 10 outcrop foot) and Tall Grass has 90 clumps, so those two are density increases. (3) Season_System.md says new games start on Spring day 1, which doesn't match Mike's "wintertime"; unverified. (4) Warmth has no activity input today.

Documented first-pass designs, all labeled "(Claude, not yet confirmed)" and for Claude Code to correct: Wood_Gathering_System.md "Ground Sticks and Fallen Branches" (about 300 stick bundles at 3 Sticks each, about 120 fallen branches at 1 Branch each, hand pickup, regrow in 5 and 7 days, same on every tier); Stone_Gathering_System.md (loose rocks roughly doubled, about 300 and 180); Trapping_System.md (Tall Grass about 200 clumps, regrow 4 to 3 days); Health_System.md "Exertion Warmth" (stamina-driven exertion level adds up to about +12°F to feels-like, slows drain but doesn't replace a fire; optional extra Hydration drain; HUD line). Difficulty_System.md and Development_Roadmap.md cross-reference all of it. Season_System.md has a "Possible mismatch" note: Claude Code should check what season a new game really starts on.

Needs Claude Code (build, after Mike confirms the numbers): ground Sticks and Branches (the Pioneer soft-lock, so most urgent), Stone and Tall Grass density, Exertion Warmth, and the start-season check. Not yet added to the "Needs Claude Code" section above; the numbers aren't confirmed. Uncommitted, along with the earlier 10-07 edits.


## 2026-10-07 — Playtest follow-up: Warmth redesigned from research (Core Temperature Model); other items accepted

Mike's replies to the playtest design notes. **Sticks and branches:** "seems ok, for now"; he'll update after continuing the playtest. **Rocks and tall grass:** "that seems good, but will confirm after playtest." Both marked "accepted for now" in Wood_Gathering_System.md, Stone_Gathering_System.md and Trapping_System.md; Claude Code should still correct anything that doesn't fit the code. **Winter start:** "it could have been spring, just the snow flurries made me think it was winter." Season_System.md note marked resolved (Claude Code can confirm Spring day 1 in seconds; nothing blocked). Difficulty_System.md and the roadmap updated to match. **Warmth from exertion:** Mike wants it as accurate to real human physiology as possible, with the body's tolerable minimum and maximum temperatures.

Research done (web): normal core temperature about 37 °C; hypothermia stages (mild 32-35 °C with shivering and confusion, moderate 28-32 °C shivering stops, severe 20-28 °C unconscious with cardiac arrest risk); heat exhaustion about 37-40 °C and heat stroke above 40 °C; thermoneutral zone about 15-24.5 °C lightly clothed and 28-32 °C unclothed; heat production in METs (walking 3.5, chopping wood 5, digging 5-6, running 10), peak shivering about 4.9 times resting; wet clothing about 5 times the conductive heat loss, wet feet about 25 times. Written up with sources and a "Not Found" list (no reliable cooling rate for a clothed person, no sourced body heat capacity, no lethal hot-side temperature) in the new `05_Documentation/Research/Human_Thermoregulation.md`. Most sources are Wikipedia and general safety pages, so numbers are approximations.

Designed (first pass, Claude, not yet confirmed): Health_System.md "Core Temperature Model", which supersedes the earlier "Exertion Warmth" idea (kept in the doc, marked superseded). Core temperature is the real value and the Warmth meter is derived from it (100 = 37 °C, 75 = 36.5, 50 = 35, 25 = 32, 0 = 28), so the existing tiers line up with real hypothermia stages and Warmth 0 matches the real collapse point. Heat balance each tick: production (rest, walking, work, sprinting by MET; shivering up to about 4.9 times resting, costing Hunger and stamina) minus loss (air, wind, wetness, clothing), exertion warming the player as a consequence. Cold side first; sweat and the hot end (Overheating, heat stroke, cause "Heat") are a second phase. Pioneer's ×1.3 is applied to heat loss. Exact rates are calibration by feel. Not added to "Needs Claude Code": the numbers aren't confirmed and it replaces tuned Warmth math. Uncommitted, along with the earlier 10-07 edits.


## 2026-10-07 — Core Temperature Model: build scope decided (cold and hot together)

Mike chose to build the temperature model's cold side and hot side together in one pass, over Claude's recommendation of cold side first. Health_System.md updated: sweat/damp state and the hot end (Overheating, heat exhaustion, heat stroke, cause "Heat") are now part of the same build; stale "second phase" wording removed. Claude Code should say whether the current weather values (summer heat, humidity) can drive the hot end. The numbers themselves are still Claude's first pass and unconfirmed by Mike, and this replaces tuned Warmth math, so per the Development Rule it waits behind the playtest. Uncommitted.

No clearing needed before the placement menus; each rebuilds its own root and keeps the original ids, so Mike's save stays valid. **Core Temperature Model review (no code changed):** buildable for the cold side with corrections, now written into Health_System.md under "Review corrections": Warmth stays a derived getter of one stored core temperature; the mapping's slope is uneven (0.02 °C per point at the top, 0.16 at the bottom); heat balance structure (resistance-based loss, ×5 on clothing resistance only, radiant campfire gain, `StepWarmth` replaced); the activity hook is missing (only `AxeTool` spends stamina); the "lightly clothed" baseline is far too harsh (core temperature would reach 35 °C in about 40 real seconds at −4 °C), so use a heavier baseline of about 2 clo; existing Hunger and Hydration cold/hot and activity multipliers double count and must be retired; heat exhaustion should start near 38 °C; shivering stops exactly at the Severe tier boundary; save format plan with `coreTempC` and old-save detection; Pioneer ×1.3 applies in the harmful direction only. **The hot side cannot be built yet:** weather has temperature only (about 34 °C at most), with no humidity, sun load, heat waves or heat Health tiers, and the difficulty temperature offset is backwards for heat. Mike's cold-and-hot-together scope therefore needs those weather additions designed first. Open for Mike: whether Claude drafts them in Weather_System.md. Damp rule, stamina link and difficulty-offset direction are Claude's new first-pass numbers, unconfirmed. Roadmap updated.

All doc edits since `66c5c53` are uncommitted.
